# LinVPN phases L0 (spikes) and L-a (foundations): implementation plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking. The spec is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-29-linvpn-design.md` (sections 1–3, 5 items 1–2, 6, 7 and 8); the earlier cross-platform spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-20-cross-platform-winvpn-design.md` and every earlier spec (vault, control API, team catalog) still apply unchanged, on Linux too. Where this plan and the spec disagree, the spec wins, except where "Resolved points" below says why the plan deviates. Follow the existing patterns of the crate you work in (read a neighbouring module first). The WinVPN 2a plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-21-winvpn-2a-foundations.md`) is the reference for rigor; `crates/platform-win` is the reference implementation of every trait.

**Goal:** L0 measures on the Ubuntu VM what the spec left open and writes one findings doc per spike: NM's OpenVPN plugin against our fixtures (L0-1) and WireGuard's handshake time without root (L0-3) are **done**; the left click on the tray icon (L0-2) is **done** and decided at CP-L0 (own SNI over `ksni`, overlay panel on Wayland); OpenVPN's challenges through an NM secret agent (L0-4) wait for the VPN lab and block nothing in L-a. A baseline of the workspace on Linux is added. L-a then delivers a testable, signed-update-ready LinVPN `.deb` (amd64 and arm64) that installs, launches, creates and unlocks the vault (auto-unlock key in the Secret Service), shows the tray icon and the card panel near the top-right corner and every window, imports, edits, exports, backs up and restores connections, subscribes to team catalogs, pairs control-API clients over a Unix socket in `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, posts notifications, follows the system's light/dark setting, starts at login, opens its file types, and updates itself through a signed `.deb` and pkexec, but connects no VPN yet: every start answers "Connecting comes in a later version of LinVPN." and System Status says so. MacVPN and WinVPN behave exactly as in 2.0.5.

**Architecture:** a new `crates/platform-linux` (`macvpn-platform-linux`) implements every `platform-api` trait for Linux, laid out like `platform-win`: `src/pure/` compiles and tests on every OS (paths, texts, the autostart file, the update rules, placement, the portal values), `src/lin/` calls the system (D-Bus through `zbus`: Secret Service, notifications, the desktop portal, logind, the single-instance name, the StatusNotifierItem; GTK through the `gtk`/`glib` 0.18 crates Tauri already brings: the clipboard, window sizes, SIGTERM) and exists only under `#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]`. The macOS control socket moves into a small shared crate `crates/unixsys` (`macvpn-unixsys`) so macOS and Linux run one implementation (Linux adds `SO_PEERCRED` and `/proc/<pid>/exe`). The `.deb` is built by `scripts/build-deb.sh` with `dpkg-deb` from Tauri's release binary plus the root update step `linvpn-install-update` (a new binary of the helper crate, the only code LinVPN runs as root in L-a). `scripts/lin-remote.sh` and `scripts/lin-smoke.sh` drive the VM from the Mac as the non-admin `linvpn-dev`.

**Tech Stack:** Rust 1.99.1 (pinned in `rust-toolchain.toml`), edition 2024, Tauri 2.12 (no Tauri bundler on Linux: `tauri build --no-bundle`), `zbus` 5 (pinned exactly when added), `gtk =0.18.2` / `glib =0.18.5` (already in `Cargo.lock` through Tauri), `ksni =0.3.7` (spike L0-2, variant S, CP-L0), SQLCipher with vendored OpenSSL (as Windows), Svelte + TS, bash, `dpkg-deb`/`dpkg-shlibdeps`, polkit (`pkexec`), Node 26.8.2 in CI.

---

## Decisions of the maintainer (2026-10-29, spec section 2) and the facts for this plan

1. **Product LinVPN**, package `linvpn`; data `~/.local/share/LinVPN`, settings `~/.config/LinVPN`, run files `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/linvpn` (each honours the XDG variable when it is set and absolute).
2. **The VM:** Ubuntu 26.04 desktop (GNOME, Wayland), non-admin user `linvpn-dev`, reached over SSH with the Mac's key `~/.ssh/linvpn_dev`; the host key is pinned in `~/.ssh/linvpn_known_hosts` (Task 1). Its address is never in the repository: `LINVPN_SSH=linvpn-dev@<VM address>`, `LINVPN_REMOTE_DIR=<dir>` (one remote dir per concurrent agent; warm dirs `linvpn-work-slot1` … `slot3` are reused, never deleted after a task).
3. **Admin steps are the maintainer's** (apt installs, installing a `.deb`, typing the polkit password at the desktop). The plan marks each one as a **checkpoint** with the exact commands, written with full command names.
4. **Backend A** (NetworkManager, no root helper for VPNs) is **confirmed** for OpenVPN by spike L0-1 (`docs/superpowers/spikes/2026-10-29-linvpn-nm-openvpn.md`). The spec (commit `ee9d4e0`) now says: connect with `AddAndActivateConnection2` and the options `persist = volatile`, `bind-activation = dbus-client`, `permissions = user:<name>` set before adding, never `nmcli connection import` (it asks for the admin password, even with `--temporary`); OpenVPN without `redirect-gateway` defaults to a split tunnel with a per-connection "Send all traffic over VPN" switch (decision 7); WireGuard liveness is the counter rule only (about 200 s; spike L0-3, `…-wireguard-liveness.md`). All of that is L-b. **The card panel** near the top-right corner; spike L0-2 is done and the maintainer decided at CP-L0 (2026-10-29): `TRAY = S` (own StatusNotifierItem over `ksni` 0.3.7; a single left or right click shows a short menu whose first item is "Open LinVPN"; a double click (`Activate`) or a middle click (`SecondaryActivate`) toggles the panel), `FORCE_X11 = false` (the panel is a transparent maximized overlay window on Wayland with the card as its input region; Xwayland only as the fallback), decision 4 stands (spec note under decision 4). Spike L0-4 (OpenVPN challenges through the secret agent) waits for the VPN lab; **no L-a task depends on it**.
5. **Releases:** a signed `.deb` per architecture, `linvpn_<version>_amd64.deb` and `linvpn_<version>_arm64.deb`, with `.sha256`, build provenance and the Ed25519 `.sig`. The signed message is `linvpn-update-v1\n<asset>\n<version>\n<sha256>\n` with the same keys as MacVPN and WinVPN. Release 2.1.0 is joint with MacVPN and WinVPN; until then the release workflow builds the `.deb`s on every tag but attaches them only when the repository variable `LINVPN_DEB_IN_RELEASE` is `true` (Task 22).
6. **Version of L-a builds:** stays `2.0.5` (the workspace version). The first released LinVPN is 2.1.0.
7. **L-b scope stays out:** the NM engines, `nm_plan`, the secret agent's VPN use, `linvpn-logd`, PackageKit installs, system VPNs, live traffic and liveness. L-a only says "engines come in L-b" through System Status' engine note.

---

**Rules for every task:**
- TDD: write the failing test first. For a pure move the "failing test" is an existing test that must stay green (name it in the commit message body). Tests that need Linux are written on the Mac and run on the VM with `scripts/lin-remote.sh` (Task 1); the step says which.
- **Targeted tests per task, the full suite per wave** (the maintainer's rule): a task runs only the crates it touched (`cargo test -p <crate> [--test <name>]`, clippy on those crates) on the Mac and, for Linux code, `scripts/lin-remote.sh run 'cargo test --locked -p <crate> …'` on the VM. The coordinator runs the full gate once per wave on the merged tip:
  - Mac: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings`, `CI=1 cargo test --workspace --locked`, `scripts/check-windows.sh`, `scripts/check-ignored.sh`, `cd app/ui && npm test && npm run build`, `MVPN_IMPL=rust scripts/test-root.sh` when `crates/helper` changed.
  - VM: `scripts/lin-remote.sh test` (the CI linux job's set, Task 1) and `scripts/lin-remote.sh run 'cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings'`.
  - Windows (only if a wave touched shared code: `crates/core`, `crates/platform-api`, `crates/app-core`, `crates/helper`, `app/src-tauri/src` outside `platform/linux*`): `scripts/win-remote.sh test`.
- **MacVPN and WinVPN unchanged:** every baseline stays green without edits: `cargo test -p macvpn-app-core --test compat_1_4`, `node scripts/l10n-baseline.mjs --check`, `cargo test -p macvpn-app --test tauri_config`, `MVPN_IMPL=rust scripts/test-root.sh`, `node scripts/third-party-notices.mjs --check` and `--os windows --check`, `cargo tree --locked --target aarch64-apple-darwin -i openssl-sys` prints nothing (and for `x86_64-apple-darwin`), and no Linux-only crate reaches a macOS or Windows binary: `cargo tree --locked -e normal --target aarch64-apple-darwin -p macvpn-app -p macvpn-helper --prefix none | grep -E '^(zbus|ksni|macvpn-platform-linux) '` prints nothing (and for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`).
- **The cfg/unsafe rule** (`crates/app-core/tests/cfg_rule.rs`): OS `cfg` only in `crates/core/src/fsx/`, `crates/platform-mac/`, `crates/platform-win/`, `crates/platform-linux/`, `crates/winsys/`, `crates/unixsys/`, `crates/helper/src/{macos,windows,linux}/`, `crates/helper/src/main.rs` and `app/src-tauri/src/platform/`; `unsafe` only in `crates/platform-mac/`, `crates/platform-win/`, `crates/platform-linux/`, `crates/winsys/`, `crates/unixsys/` and `crates/helper/`. Task 9 is the only task that edits the allow-lists.
- **Dependencies:** every Linux dependency is a `[target.'cfg(target_os = "linux")'.dependencies]` entry pinned exactly (`=x.y.z`); `gtk =0.18.2`, `glib =0.18.5`, `libc 0.2.191` are already in `Cargo.lock`; a new crate (`zbus` and what it pulls in, `ksni` if chosen) is listed in the Linux notices (Task 21). No new crate reaches a macOS or Windows binary.
- **Secrets:** never log, and never put in argv, env, a window title, a notification or a D-Bus property: a password, a PSK, a key password, an OTP, a recovery key, the master password, a vault key (DEK), a team key, an admin key, a bearer token, a subscription string, a control-API token, or a config's content. Zeroize every buffer that held one (`Zeroizing`), including the Secret Service reply's copy.
- **Fail closed:** a check that cannot be completed (an `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` that is not ours and 0700, a peer whose credentials cannot be read, a `.deb` whose fields cannot be read, a signature that cannot be checked) refuses the operation.
- Serialized formats are frozen (vault, backup, export, catalog, `control-clients.json`, `connection-order.json`, `system-otp.json`, `meta.json`, view-model JSON, IPC payloads). A new view-model field is `#[serde(skip_serializing_if = …)]` and absent on macOS, so the compat fixtures stay byte-identical.
- Every new user-facing string goes into `crates/core/l10n/strings.json` in EN and HU with the right `scope`; a Linux-only text has `"only": ["linux"]` and names LinVPN directly (as Windows-only texts name WinVPN). Tasks that add strings only add entries (shared file: merge conflicts are resolved by keeping both entries, sorted).
- Shell instructions for the maintainer use full command names (`systemctl`, never `sc`; `apt-get install`, never an alias).
- Nothing in L-a writes to NetworkManager (no connection is added, even in a test or a spike step of this plan, except Task 6 when it runs, under spike L0-1's polkit rule).
- Long remote commands go through `scripts/lin-remote.sh` (it runs them detached with a remote log and polls it: an SSH session may hang). Never run a long command in a bare `ssh`.
- Commit trailer: `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`. Never push.
- The maintainer's company name never enters the repo, nor any VM or LAN address. Use "Office VPN", "Acme VPN", `vpn.example.com`, `192.0.2.0/24` (TEST-NET-1) in examples.
- Never touch the Mac's real Keychain or `~/Library/Application Support/MacVPN`; on the VM, tests use their own temp directories, a private D-Bus session (`dbus-run-session`) and uniquely named test items (`local.linvpn.test.<uuid>`), which they delete. Only the tests marked `desktop:` (ignored everywhere, listed in `scripts/ignored-everywhere.txt`) touch the real desktop session, and only at a checkpoint.

**Parallel execution.** Tasks in the same wave touch disjoint files except where a task says "shared with". Run each parallel task in its own worktree (superpowers:using-git-worktrees) from the tip of the previous wave; merge in task order; the next wave starts from the merged tip. Each worktree uses its own remote dir on the VM (`LINVPN_REMOTE_DIR=linvpn-work-slot<N>`), so parallel tasks never overwrite each other's checkout or build. Shared files within a wave: `crates/core/l10n/strings.json` (entries only), `crates/platform-linux/{Cargo.toml,src/lib.rs,src/pure/mod.rs,src/lin/mod.rs}` (each task adds its own lines), `crates/platform-linux/src/lin/platform.rs` (each task replaces only its own accessor's body), `scripts/ignored-everywhere.txt` (lines only).

**Maintainer checkpoints** (each blocks only the step that names it; the coordinator tells the maintainer the exact text below and waits):
- **CP0 VM ready** (before Task 1 Step 6): the VM, `linvpn-dev`, the SSH key and the pinned host key exist (used by the spikes); Task 1 Step 1 checks the build dependencies and NM plugins and asks the maintainer only for what is missing.
- **CP-L0-2 clicks** (Task 4 Step 4): the maintainer is at the VM's desktop and performs the scripted clicks.
- **CP-lab** (Task 6 Step 3, whenever the maintainer starts the lab; blocks nothing in L-a): the VPN lab runs on the Mac and the VM reaches it.
- **CP-L0** (after Task 7): the maintainer reads the baseline and tray findings and confirms the tray variant, `FORCE_X11` and decision 4 (decision 3 is already confirmed by L0-1). **Done 2026-10-29:** `TRAY = S`, `FORCE_X11 = false`, decision 4 stands (Task 7).
- **CP-desktop login** (Task 23 Step 6 and every later smoke run): `linvpn-dev` is logged in at the VM's desktop (the screen may lock).
- **CP-deb install** (Task 21 Step 8): the maintainer installs the built `.deb` on the VM.
- **CP-update** (Task 25 Step 5): the maintainer signs a throwaway package with a dry run and approves one pkexec prompt at the desktop.

**Key facts from the current code (read before starting):**
- **Workspace:** members `crates/{core,platform-api,platform-mac,platform-win,winsys,helper,app-core}`, `app/src-tauri`, `examples/macvpn-control-client`; version `2.0.5`; `[workspace.lints.rust] unsafe_code = "deny"`. `rust-toolchain.toml` lists the two macOS and two Windows targets. `Cargo.lock` already has `gtk 0.18.2`, `glib 0.18.5`, `gio 0.18.4`, `gdk 0.18.2`, `webkit2gtk 2.0.2`, `tray-icon 0.25.1`, `libappindicator 0.9.0`, `muda 0.20.0`, `tao 0.37.1` (which brings `dbus 0.9.12`), `libc 0.2.191`, `sha2 0.10.9/0.11.1`, `ed25519-dalek 3.0.0`; no `zbus`, no `ksni`, no `signal-hook`.
- **Linux today:** `TargetOs::Linux` exists (`crates/core/src/target_os.rs`; `host()` maps every non-macOS, non-Windows OS to it). `Product::update_asset` returns `None` for Linux. `crates/core/Cargo.toml` has **no** `rusqlite` for Linux (macOS: `bundled-sqlcipher`, Windows: `bundled-sqlcipher-vendored-openssl`), so the `vault` feature does not compile on Linux. `app/src-tauri/src/platform/mod.rs` has `compile_error!("no platform layer for this operating system yet (Linux is phase 4)")`; `crates/helper/src/main.rs` runs the Windows backend on any other OS. The OpenVPN validator has a draft `PLATFORM_LINUX` tier (L-b reviews it).
- **l10n:** `strings.json` entries `{hu, scope, variants?: {windows|linux: {en, hu}}, only?: [...]}`. 212 entries carry a `windows` variant. `crates/app-core/tests/l10n_coverage.rs::macos_terms_are_classified` requires `only` or a `windows` variant for every text naming a macOS term; `no_macos_term_reaches_windows` checks the resolved Windows texts. `crates/core/tests/l10n.rs` has a `LINUX` text platform constant (`product: "LinVPN"`). The UI's `l10n-core.ts` already knows `linux`.
- **The update signature** (`crates/core/src/update.rs`, "MARK: - Signature"): `SignatureDomain::{MacVpnV1, WinVpnV1}`, `UpdateSignature::{DOMAIN, WINVPN_DOMAIN, message, message_for, verify, verify_for, verify_trusted, verify_trusted_for}`; tests in `crates/core/tests/update.rs` (`winvpn_*`, `released_macvpn_signatures_still_verify`, the `sign-release.sh --dry-run --msi` test). `scripts/sign-release.sh` signs `MacVPN-<v>.dmg` and both `WinVPN-<v>-<arch>.msi` ("both or neither"); `scripts/test-sign-release.sh` prints Hungarian.
- **platform-api:** `Platform` (one accessor per facility), `Product { name, os, app_id, repo, user_guide_stem, signature_domain }` with `MACVPN`, `WINVPN`; `ControlTransport { bind, random_32, is_already_closed, prepare_dir }` (Windows binds a named pipe that is not in `dir`: the contract allows an endpoint outside `dir`); `SystemSetup::engine_note()` and `SetupReport::engine_missing` (WinVPN 2a's "not yet" state); `WindowChrome::{tray_tint, window_corner_radius, set_outer_frame}`; `AppLifecycle::{observe_appearance, observe_sleep}`; `FilePreferences` (JSON file, portable); the shared curl layer `platform_api::curl` (`Curl { executable, extra_args, private_dir, temp_base }`, `CurlHttpClient`, `CurlCatalogFetcher`).
- **platform-win** is the model: `src/pure/` (every OS) and `src/win/` (Windows), `WinPlatform` in `src/win/platform.rs` (every trait), the "not yet" texts in `pure/texts.rs`, the updater split `pure/update.rs` / `win/updater.rs`, the update source compiled in (`pure/update_check.rs`, `UPDATE_REPO_ID = 1_422_472_799`), dates in `pure/dates.rs`.
- **The control socket** is `crates/platform-mac/src/control_socket.rs` (282 lines: `prepare_dir`, `bind` with `macvpn.lock` + `flock`, `with_umask`, `peer` via `getpeereid` + `LOCAL_PEERPID` + `proc_pidpath`, `same_user`, `is_already_closed`, `random_32`; `SUN_PATH_LEN = 104`), used by `crates/platform-mac/src/api/control.rs` and tested by `crates/platform-mac/tests/{control_socket,control_socket_umask}.rs`. The app puts the control dir at `<data dir>/control` (`app/src-tauri/src/control.rs::ControlApp::new`), which also holds the audit file. The example client's `default_socket_path()` is the macOS path; its `cfg`s are outside the scanned roots.
- **The app:** `main.rs` order: `platform::harden_dll_search`, `helper_mode`, `relaunch`, `supported`, `claim_instance`, `init(sandbox)`, `install_core_hooks`, `Environment::detect`; setup calls `configure_app`, `install_terminate_handler`, `observe_wake`, `appearance::apply`, `tray::create_menu_window`, `tray::create`. `tray.rs` builds Tauri's `TrayIconBuilder`, places the popover by `popover_position(icon rect, size, monitor)` and hides it on blur (`hide_on_blur`). `appearance.rs` maps `Appearance::System` to `None` (the toolkit follows the system). `fit.rs` calls `WindowChrome::fit_viewport` with `platform::native_window(&w)`.
- **The smoke run:** `scripts/smoke-manage.sh` (554 lines; `SMOKE_OS` from `uname -s`: `MINGW*|MSYS*` → windows, else macos), the JS steps in `scripts/smoke/*.js` (they ask `invoke('get_platform').os`), the driver `app/src-tauri/src/platform/smoke/{mod.rs,macos.rs,ras.rs,features.rs}` with `cfg(target_os = "macos")` / `cfg(windows)` branches. `scripts/win-smoke.sh` runs it in `winvpn-dev`'s desktop session through a one-shot scheduled task; `scripts/test-win-smoke.sh` tests it in print mode.
- **CI** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`): macOS jobs `rust` (also runs `test-sign-release.sh`, `test-win-remote.sh`, `test-win-smoke.sh`, the bash syntax check of `scripts/*.sh`), `node`, `dmg`; Windows jobs `windows`, `msi`, `windows-admin`. **Release** (`.github/workflows/release.yml`): `ci-green`, `build` (DMG), `build-windows` (MSIs, attached only with `WINVPN_MSI_IN_RELEASE == 'true'`), `release` (no checkout; provenance; `gh release create --draft`); comments in Hungarian.
- **The VM** (spikes L0-1 and L0-3, 2026-10-29): Ubuntu 26.04.1, x86_64 (`amd64`), NetworkManager 1.54.3, network-manager-openvpn 1.12.5, OpenVPN 2.7.0; `linvpn-dev` is uid 1001; `~/.ssh/linvpn_dev` and `~/.ssh/linvpn_known_hosts` exist on the Mac. The spikes ran over plain `ssh $LINVPN_SSH` and `systemd-run --user`; there is no `scripts/lin-remote.sh` yet (Task 1). Their throwaway scripts are in `docs/superpowers/spikes/2026-10-29-linvpn-l0-a/`.
- **Polkit as `linvpn-dev`** (spike L0-3's table): from an SSH shell every NM write asks for authentication; from the desktop session or the user manager (`systemd-run --user`) adding or deleting a connection **with** `permissions = user:<me>`, activating and setting the statistics rate need no prompt, while a connection **without** permissions asks for the admin password on the desktop. Rule for every tool and test: NM writes only through the user manager, only after a non-interactive `CheckAuthorization` (flags 0), and never without `permissions`. L-a writes nothing to NM.
- **The VPN lab:** `scripts/vpn-lab/profiles.sh` generates the PKI and the client profiles without Docker (`--bind IPV4 [--host NAME]`, state in `$LAB_STATE`, default `scripts/vpn-lab/.state`, git-ignored); `up.sh` starts the servers (Docker, the maintainer's). `client/macos/openvpn-static-challenge.ovpn` is the static challenge (user `labotp`); any other OpenVPN profile with `labotp` is the dynamic CRV1 challenge; the code is in `client/CREDENTIALS.txt`.
- **OpenVPN fixtures:** `Tests/Fixtures/{mikrotik,unifi,pfsense,opnsense}/*.ovpn` (14 files incl. `pfsense/archive/pfSense-UDP4-1194-alice.ovpn` with its `…-tls.key`).

## Resolved points (where the spec leaves a choice open, and where this plan deviates)

- **One Unix-socket implementation: a new crate `crates/unixsys`.** "Reuse the macOS code" for the control socket: `platform-linux` cannot depend on `platform-mac` (it is `#![cfg(target_os = "macos")]` as a whole), and a copy would duplicate the audited code. So `control_socket.rs` moves into `macvpn-unixsys` (`#![cfg(unix)]`, safe public API, `unsafe` only for the libc calls), with the peer lookup per OS: macOS keeps `getpeereid` + `LOCAL_PEERPID` + `proc_pidpath` byte for byte; Linux uses `getsockopt(SO_PEERCRED)` and `read_link("/proc/<pid>/exe")` (a target ending in ` (deleted)` is `None`: such a peer cannot pair). `platform-mac` keeps `pub mod control_socket { pub use macvpn_unixsys::control_socket::*; }`, so every caller and the moved tests stay unchanged. `SUN_PATH_LEN` is 104 on macOS, 108 on Linux.
- **The control socket's place:** `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/linvpn/control/linvpn.sock` (spec 3.1), not under the data dir. The Linux `ControlTransport::bind(dir)` ignores `dir` for the socket (as Windows' pipe does) and binds in the runtime dir; `prepare_dir(dir)` still prepares `<data dir>/control` for the audit file. `socket_file` is the socket path (removed at stop), `display` is that path. `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` must be absolute, a real directory owned by this uid with mode 0700, else the control API stays off with the error shown (fail closed). The sandbox uses `<sandbox>/run/linvpn` (created 0700). The example client's `default_socket_path()` on Linux returns the same path.
- **D-Bus through `zbus` 5** (the spec's choice), blocking API, default features (its own `async-io` executor; no tokio coupling). Rejected: `gio` 0.18's GDBus (already in the tree, but gtk3-rs is unmaintained and it would tie every D-Bus call to the GTK main loop) and `dbus` 0.9 (libdbus bindings; C, and no maintained SNI on top). One session and one system connection per process (`lin::bus`), created lazily; the sandbox platform never opens one at construction (tests create no bus).
- **The vault key in the Secret Service: plain `zbus`, no `secret-service` / `oo7` crate.** The calls needed are few (`OpenSession("plain")`, `SearchItems`, `Unlock` with its `Prompt`, `GetSecret`, `CreateItem` with `replace`, `Item.Delete`), all over the `zbus` connection the platform needs anyway. The `oo7` and `secret-service` crates bring their own crypto stacks (the DH-AES session) and a file backend we do not use. The DH session only protects the secret on the session bus, which only this user's processes reach: the same-user boundary the macOS Keychain without per-app ACL and the Windows Credential Manager already accept (WinVPN decision 1). The item: default collection (alias `default`), label `LinVPN vault key`, attributes `application = local.linvpn`, `kind = vault-key` (literals in platform-linux, never derived from `Product`). The DEK copies are `Zeroizing`. A locked collection: `Unlock` → the system's own prompt (`Prompt.Prompt("")`, wait for `Completed`, at most 120 s); dismissed → `Unavailable`; no Secret Service on the bus → `Unavailable` (the vault then asks for the master password, as macOS does without the Keychain).
- **SQLCipher on Linux: `bundled-sqlcipher-vendored-openssl`, as Windows.** One crypto build for both non-macOS products, statically linked; no dependency on the distribution's `libssl3` / `libssl3t64` package names; the release gate checks the binary links no `libssl`, `libcrypto` or `libsqlite3` (`readelf -d`). `libssl-dev` on the VM is only Tauri's listed prerequisite.
- **The `.deb` is built by `scripts/build-deb.sh` with `dpkg-deb`, not by Tauri's deb bundler.** Tauri builds the release binary (`tauri build --no-bundle`); the script stages the tree, writes `DEBIAN/control` (Package `linvpn` exactly, Version, Architecture from `dpkg --print-architecture`, Depends from `dpkg-shlibdeps` plus the fixed list, Recommends), and runs `dpkg-deb --build --root-owner-group`. Reasons: the package name must be `linvpn` byte for byte (the updater checks it), `Recommends` and the extra files (MIME XML, polkit action, the root update step) must be exact, and every file is gated. **No maintainer scripts:** dpkg's file triggers (shared-mime-info, desktop-file-utils, hicolor's icon cache) do everything a `postinst` would; the gate refuses any `preinst`/`postinst`/`prerm`/`postrm`. The user's data and autostart file stay on removal (Debian policy; the user guide says how to remove them).
- **The update's root step is a small binary of its own, `/usr/libexec/linvpn/linvpn-install-update`** (a second `[[bin]]` of the helper crate, its code in `crates/helper/src/linux/`), started by `pkexec` under the polkit action `local.linvpn.update` (`auth_admin`, message "LinVPN wants to install its update."). Not `pkexec apt-get install ./<file>.deb` directly, and not a mode of the GUI binary: (a) running `apt-get` directly would install whatever file is at the path when root reads it (a same-user process could swap it after the app's check) and the prompt would name `/usr/bin/apt-get`; (b) the GUI binary as root would load GTK and WebKit as root. The step copies the file into a fresh root-owned 0700 directory, then on the copy: verifies the Ed25519 signature itself (`UpdateSignature::verify_trusted_for(LinVpnV1, asset, version, sha256(copy), sig)`), checks `Package: linvpn`, `Architecture` = `dpkg --print-architecture`, `Version` = the signed version and newer than the installed one, and only then runs `apt-get install -y ./<copy>`. So root never trusts the user's file or the app's check. The app still verifies first (for the user's messages). The pure rules (asset names, field parsing, version order, exit codes) live in `macvpn_core::deb_update` (core, no `cfg`), shared by the app side and the root step.
- **After the update:** `pkexec` returns; the app writes the outcome notice (as WinVPN's relauncher does) into its private updates folder, starts `/usr/bin/linvpn --after-update` detached (`setsid`), and quits (`UpdateOutcome::HelperLaunched`). The new process waits up to 20 s for the single-instance name to be released, then starts normally and shows the notice. `pkexec` exit 126 (dialog dismissed) or 127 (not authorized) → `UpdateOutcome::Cancelled`, nothing installed.
- **Single instance: the session-bus name `local.linvpn.Instance`** (`RequestName` with `DO_NOT_QUEUE`); the object `/local/linvpn/Instance`, interface `local.linvpn.Instance1`, method `Open(as files)`; a second launch calls `Open` with its absolute file arguments (or none: "show yourself", the same decision as Windows: `open_files::show_yourself`) and exits 0. The sandbox owns `local.linvpn.Sandbox.h<first 16 hex digits of SHA-256 of the sandbox path>`, so a smoke run never collides with an installed LinVPN. The session bus is the user's alone (same trust as macOS' `open -a`); the download mark check still applies to every file.
- **Single-instance failure** (no session bus): LinVPN runs anyway and logs it (a desktop without a session bus has no tray either; fail open here is safe: the control socket's lock still keeps one control server per user).
- **Tray: decided by spike L0-2,** implemented once in Task 17 with two concrete variants: **(S) an own StatusNotifierItem over `ksni`** with `ItemIsMenu = false` (left click → `Activate` → the panel) and a menu (right click) whose first item is "Open LinVPN" (so a host that opens the menu on left click still reaches the panel in one more click), or **(T) Tauri's tray icon** with that menu as its only interaction (Tauri's Linux tray delivers no click events). Task 17 says which steps belong to which variant; the spike's findings name the variant. No third path. **Decided at CP-L0 (2026-10-29): variant S.** Ubuntu's AppIndicators extension never sends `Activate` on a single left click (it opens the menu after the 400 ms double-click wait); a double click sends `Activate`, a middle click `SecondaryActivate`; both toggle the panel. So the menu's "Open LinVPN" is the main single-click path on GNOME (the maintainer accepts the delay).
- **Panel placement:** the panel opens at the top-right corner of the work area of the monitor under the pointer (or the primary monitor), 8 px from the right edge and 8 px below the work area's top (below GNOME's top bar), and hides on focus loss. GNOME on Wayland ignores client positioning; spike L0-2 measures it and decides `FORCE_X11` (Task 17): when native Wayland does not honour the position, LinVPN asks GDK for its X11 backend (XWayland) by setting `GDK_BACKEND=x11` in `platform-linux::prefer_x11_backend()`, called first in `main` before any thread exists (`std::env::set_var` is `unsafe` in edition 2024, so it lives in the platform crate). A user's own `GDK_BACKEND` is respected. **Decided at CP-L0 (2026-10-29): `FORCE_X11 = false`.** Spike L0-2 found Mutter centres every positioned Wayland toplevel (every type hint) and GNOME has no layer shell, but a **transparent maximized overlay window** works: the window is undecorated, transparent and maximized (Mutter fills the work area of its monitor), the page draws the card at the top-right (8 px in, 8 px below the top bar), and the GDK window's input region is the card's rectangle (`gdk_window_input_shape_combine_region`), re-applied in a `size-allocate` handler because GtkWindow resets the input shape on every allocation. A click outside passes through and the focus loss hides the panel. Xwayland (`prefer_x11_backend()` with the explicit position above) is only the fallback if the overlay fails on real hardware; it is not built in L-a unless Task 17/18 find the overlay broken.
- **Window corners:** square on Linux in L-a (`popover_corner_radius() = 0.0`, `round_corners` returns `false`); the L-c parity audit decides whether to draw rounded corners in a transparent window.
- **Appearance:** GTK 3 and WebKitGTK do not follow the portal's `color-scheme` by themselves, so `WindowChrome::system_dark()` (new, default `None` = the toolkit follows the system: macOS, Windows) returns the portal's value on Linux (`org.freedesktop.portal.Settings.ReadOne("org.freedesktop.appearance", "color-scheme")`: 1 → `Some(true)`, 2 → `Some(false)`, 0 or unreadable → `None`), `appearance.rs` maps `Appearance::System` to that theme, and `observe_appearance` (the portal's `SettingChanged`) re-applies it.
- **File panels: the desktop portal's `org.freedesktop.portal.FileChooser`** (`OpenFile`, `SaveFile`; the `Response` signal on the returned request), with an empty parent window id (the panel is not modal; accepted for L-a, the L-c audit may add the `wayland:`/`x11:` handle). `done` (not `Send`) is queued for the GTK main thread through `glib::MainContext::default().invoke_local`-style dispatch from the app's main thread (`run_on_main_thread`), as on Windows.
- **Secret clipboard:** GTK's clipboard (`gtk 0.18`) on the main thread: the text plus the target `x-kde-passwordManagerHint` = `secret` (honoured by KDE's Klipper and by GNOME clipboard extensions that implement it); `ClipboardMark` counts the clipboard's `owner-change` signals; `clear_if_unchanged` clears only while the count is still the mark.
- **Download mark:** the extended attribute `user.xdg.origin.url` that browsers set (`getxattr`). Present → marked; `ENODATA` → not marked; `ENOTSUP` (a file system without user xattrs, where no mark can exist) → not marked; a missing file → not marked; any other error → marked (fail closed, as the trait says).
- **Quit and logout:** SIGTERM (systemd stops the user's session scope at logout with SIGTERM) and SIGINT through `glib::unix_signal_add_local` on the GTK main loop (= Tauri's main thread on Linux) call the terminate handler: `Now` → exit; `Later` → the app's quit flow runs and exits on `reply_to_terminate(true)`; `Cancel` → logged, the app stays (logout follows with SIGKILL after systemd's stop timeout). GNOME's `org.gnome.SessionManager.Inhibit` (a logout dialog entry for unsaved changes) is left to the L-c audit. Sleep and wake: logind's `PrepareForSleep(bool)` on the system bus.
- **Launch at login:** `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/autostart/linvpn.desktop` (default `~/.config/autostart`), `Exec=/usr/bin/linvpn --autostart`. On: the file exists, has no `Hidden=true` and no `X-GNOME-Autostart-enabled=false`. A file turned off by another tool shows the note "Turned off by another app (Startup Applications)." and the switch turns it on again by rewriting the whole file. GNOME has no settings page for it: `shows_open_settings = false`, `open_settings` does nothing. A development build (not `/usr/bin/linvpn`) cannot toggle (`can_toggle = false`, note "Launch at login is available when LinVPN is installed from its package."), as macOS outside `/Applications`. The sandbox uses the no-op item.
- **Notifications:** `org.freedesktop.Notifications.Notify` with app name `LinVPN`, `desktop-entry` hint `linvpn`, the action `default` (a click: `ActionInvoked(id, "default")` → `on_click` with the user info); `remove_delivered` closes the id posted for that identifier (`CloseNotification`). The identifier → id map is kept in memory. The body never carries a secret (rule above).
- **Preferences:** `FilePreferences` at `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/LinVPN/preferences.json` (private dir 0700, file 0600), as WinVPN does under its data dir. The sandbox keeps memory preferences.
- **Dates and collation:** dates through `localtime_r` (libc) formatted by the shared pure date code, which moves from `platform-win/src/pure/dates.rs` to `platform-api/src/dates.rs` (WinVPN re-exports it; its tests move with it). Collation: the core's built-in comparison (no ICU dependency); the L-c audit checks Hungarian sorting. Preferred languages: `LANGUAGE` (colon list), else `LC_ALL`, `LC_MESSAGES`, `LANG` (`hu_HU.UTF-8` → `hu-HU`), parsed in `pure::languages`.
- **What answers "not yet" in L-a:** `HelperClient::status` → `Ok(vec![])`, `up`/`down`/`respond` → the localized "Connecting comes in a later version of LinVPN."; `NativeController::list` → `Ok(vec![])`, `start`/`stop` → the same text; `NativeProfiles::build` → `Ok` with an empty profile, `install` → `Err(` the same text `)`; `SystemVpnWatch::start` → an inert guard; `SystemSetup::check` → a report with `engine_missing = Some(` the same text `)`; `engine_note()` → "Connecting comes in a later version of LinVPN: its engines arrive with NetworkManager support. You can already create, import, edit, export and back up connections."; `NetworkProbe` → `NoNetworkProbe`; the log stream → `Unsupported`. HTTPS catalogs, the release source and the updater work (the shared curl layer over `/usr/bin/curl`; `curl` is in `Depends`).
- **Tests that need a session bus** run under `dbus-run-session` (an isolated bus; CI and `lin-remote.sh test` wrap the whole test command in it) against fakes the tests serve themselves (a fake Secret Service, a fake notification server, a fake portal). Tests against the real desktop (gnome-keyring, the notification popup, the portal) are `#[ignore = "desktop: …"]`, listed in `scripts/ignored-everywhere.txt`, and run at a checkpoint with `scripts/lin-remote.sh desktop 'cargo test … -- --ignored'`.
- **The VM's desktop context:** processes started over SSH belong to the SSH login session (remote: every NM write asks for authentication); the smoke and the desktop tests need the desktop. `lin-remote.sh desktop 'CMD'` and `lin-smoke.sh` start the command with `systemd-run --user` in `linvpn-dev`'s user manager, which GNOME fills with `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, `DISPLAY` and the session bus, and which polkit counts as the user's graphical session (confirmed by spikes L0-1 and L0-3).
- **CI builds on `ubuntu-24.04` (amd64) and `ubuntu-24.04-arm` (arm64), natively**, not cross-compiled (WebKitGTK and GTK cross builds need a full arm64 sysroot). Building on 24.04 makes the `.deb` run on Ubuntu 24.04+ and Debian 13 (glibc 2.39); the VM (26.04) is the test system. The VM's own architecture (recorded at CP0) is the only one tested by hand; the other is built and gated in CI.
- **No `scripts/check-linux.sh` on the Mac.** Unlike Windows, every Linux crate needs C code built for Linux (SQLCipher, GTK, WebKit); the VM and CI check it.
- **File associations:** `.ovpn`, `.sstp` and `.macvpncatalog`, exactly what MacVPN (Info.plist) and WinVPN claim. The spec's list also names `.conf` and `.macvpnbackup`: MacVPN claims `.conf` only as an alternate handler and claims no backup type; on Linux `.conf` is `text/plain`, so a MIME claim would offer LinVPN for every text file, and there is no "Open with only" for a MIME type. So `.conf` and backups are not claimed (users import them from the app, as on macOS); `.ovpn` uses shared-mime-info's `application/x-openvpn-profile`, the other two get LinVPN's MIME XML (Task 21).
- **The update's root step and spec section 8** ("no root component except `linvpn-logd`"): the spec's own update design (section 7) already runs apt as root through pkexec; the root step replaces that bare `apt-get` with a checked one. It is no resident component: it runs once per approved update and exits.
- **A StatusNotifierItem host as a dependency:** `Depends: gnome-shell-extension-appindicator | plasma-workspace` (Ubuntu ships the extension enabled; KDE has its own host). Other desktops satisfy it by installing the extension package, which does nothing outside GNOME; the user guide says the tray needs an SNI host.
- **The smoke run on Linux** runs every section except what is AppKit's or RAS's: the Dock icon, the screenshots and the appearance print say "skipped on Linux"; the popover's corners are square (no check); the system's quit request is SIGTERM, which the driver sends to its own process (`macvpn_platform_linux::smoke::send_terminate`); there is no old Keychain item and no Unix-mode difference (Linux checks modes with `stat -c %a`). `SMOKE_LANG` sets `LANGUAGE` for the run (the real path of `pure::languages`). The smoke's System Status report is the driver's stand-in for L-b's engines (every kind available through the stand-in helper), so the connect flows of the JS steps run unchanged.

# Part 1 — L0: the VM harness and the spikes

## Task 1: the VM harness (`scripts/lin-remote.sh`)

**Goal:** every later task can sync the working tree to the VM, build and test there as `linvpn-dev`, run a command in the desktop session, and survive a hanging SSH session: long commands run detached with a remote log that the Mac polls.

**Files:**
- Create: `scripts/lin-remote.sh`, `scripts/test-lin-remote.sh`
- Modify: `docs/architecture.md` (a new section "Checking Linux" with the subsection "The Linux test machine", after "Checking Windows")
- Modify: `.gitignore` (the spikes' build output)

- [ ] **Step 1: CP0 — the VM's build dependencies**

The VM, the user, the SSH key and the pinned host key exist (the spikes used them). Check what the build needs:

Run: `ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o IdentitiesOnly=yes -i ~/.ssh/linvpn_dev -o UserKnownHostsFile=~/.ssh/linvpn_known_hosts "$LINVPN_SSH" 'dpkg-query -W -f="\${Package} \${Status}\n" build-essential libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev libayatana-appindicator3-dev librsvg2-dev libxdo-dev libssl-dev nodejs npm wireguard-tools network-manager-openvpn-gnome network-manager-l2tp-gnome network-manager-strongswan network-manager-sstp-gnome network-manager-vpnc-gnome git curl dbus-user-session 2>&1; node --version'`
Expected: every package `install ok installed`, Node 22.12 or newer. If anything is missing, send the maintainer the block below (only the missing packages in the `apt-get` line) and wait; skip the user and key part, which is done.

> **CP0 VM ready.** On the Ubuntu 26.04 VM, as your admin user, in a terminal:
>
> ```bash
> sudo apt-get update
> sudo apt-get install --yes build-essential libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev libayatana-appindicator3-dev librsvg2-dev \
>   libxdo-dev libssl-dev nodejs npm wireguard-tools network-manager-openvpn-gnome network-manager-l2tp-gnome \
>   network-manager-strongswan network-manager-sstp-gnome network-manager-vpnc-gnome \
>   openssh-server git curl dbus-user-session
> sudo adduser --gecos "" linvpn-dev
> sudo install --directory --mode=700 --owner=linvpn-dev --group=linvpn-dev /home/linvpn-dev/.ssh
> ```
>
> (`network-manager-strongswan` has no separate `-gnome` package: it contains the GNOME dialog. `openssh-server`,
> `git`, `curl` and `dbus-user-session` are needed for the harness; skip the ones already installed.) Do **not** add
> `linvpn-dev` to the `sudo` group. On the Mac, if `~/.ssh/linvpn_dev` does not exist yet:
>
> ```bash
> ssh-keygen -t ed25519 -f ~/.ssh/linvpn_dev -C linvpn-dev
> ```
>
> Copy `~/.ssh/linvpn_dev.pub` to the VM (for example on a USB stick or a shared folder) and install it:
>
> ```bash
> sudo install --mode=600 --owner=linvpn-dev --group=linvpn-dev linvpn_dev.pub /home/linvpn-dev/.ssh/authorized_keys
> ssh-keygen -l -f /etc/ssh/ssh_host_ed25519_key.pub
> dpkg --print-architecture
> node --version
> ```
>
> Then on the Mac pin the host key and compare its fingerprint with the one the VM printed:
>
> ```bash
> ssh-keyscan -t ed25519 <VM address> > ~/.ssh/linvpn_known_hosts
> ssh-keygen -l -f ~/.ssh/linvpn_known_hosts
> ```
>
> Tell the coordinator: the fingerprints match, the VM's architecture (`amd64` or `arm64`) and the Node version
> (it must be 22.12 or newer). Keep the address out of the repository: the coordinator uses
> `LINVPN_SSH=linvpn-dev@<VM address>`.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write `scripts/test-lin-remote.sh` (the failing test)**

```bash
#!/bin/bash
# Tests scripts/lin-remote.sh without a VM: LINVPN_REMOTE_PRINT=1 prints each remote script instead of running it
# over SSH (nothing is copied, nothing waits). Checks the job wrapper (detached, logged, exit code recorded), that
# `test` runs the CI linux job's set under dbus-run-session with CI=1, that `desktop` goes through systemd-run --user
# after the desktop-session check, and the argument checks.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
SCRIPT="$PWD/scripts/lin-remote.sh"

PASS=0; FAIL=0
ok()  { PASS=$((PASS+1)); echo "ok   - $1"; }
nok() { FAIL=$((FAIL+1)); echo "FAIL - $1"; }
check() { local desc="$1"; shift; if "$@"; then ok "$desc"; else nok "$desc"; fi; }
has() { grep -qF -- "$2" <<<"$1"; }
lacks() { ! grep -qF -- "$2" <<<"$1"; }
before() { local a b; a=$(grep -nF -- "$2" <<<"$1" | head -1 | cut -d: -f1); b=$(grep -nF -- "$3" <<<"$1" | head -1 | cut -d: -f1); [ -n "$a" ] && [ -n "$b" ] && [ "$a" -lt "$b" ]; }

check "bash -n" bash -n "$SCRIPT"
if command -v shellcheck >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  check "shellcheck" shellcheck "$SCRIPT" "$0"
else
  echo "skip - shellcheck is not installed"
fi

print() { LINVPN_SSH=linvpn-dev@example.invalid LINVPN_REMOTE_DIR=linvpn-work-slot9 LINVPN_REMOTE_PRINT=1 "$SCRIPT" "$@" 2>&1; }

check "usage without arguments" bash -c "! '$SCRIPT' >/dev/null 2>&1"
check "needs LINVPN_SSH" bash -c "! env -u LINVPN_SSH LINVPN_REMOTE_PRINT=1 '$SCRIPT' sync 2>&1 | grep -q 'git fetch'"
check "refuses a bad LINVPN_REMOTE_DIR" bash -c "! LINVPN_SSH=a@b LINVPN_REMOTE_DIR='x;rm' LINVPN_REMOTE_PRINT=1 '$SCRIPT' sync >/dev/null 2>&1"
check "refuses an unknown command" bash -c "! LINVPN_SSH=a@b LINVPN_REMOTE_PRINT=1 '$SCRIPT' frobnicate >/dev/null 2>&1"

SYNC="$(print sync)"
check "sync: says print mode copies nothing" has "$SYNC" "print only: nothing is synced"

TEST="$(print test)"
check "test: syncs first" has "$TEST" "print only: nothing is synced"
check "test: the CI linux job's set" has "$TEST" "cargo test --locked -p macvpn-core -p macvpn-app-core -p macvpn-platform-api"
check "test: under a private session bus" has "$TEST" "dbus-run-session -- cargo test"
check "test: CI=1" has "$TEST" "export CI=1"
check "test: detached, not in the SSH session" has "$TEST" "setsid --fork"
check "test: the job's log" has "$TEST" ".lin-jobs/"
check "test: the exit code is recorded" has "$TEST" '.rc.tmp'
check "test: never --target (the VM's own architecture)" lacks "$TEST" "--target"

RUN="$(print run 'cargo build --locked -p macvpn-platform-linux')"
check "run: syncs first" has "$RUN" "print only: nothing is synced"
check "run: the command in the job script" has "$RUN" "cargo build --locked -p macvpn-platform-linux"
NOSYNC="$(print run-nosync 'echo polled')"
check "run-nosync: does not sync" lacks "$NOSYNC" "nothing is synced"
check "run-nosync: runs the command" has "$NOSYNC" "echo polled"

DESK="$(print desktop 'cargo test --locked -p macvpn-platform-linux -- --ignored')"
check "desktop: checks the graphical session first" has "$DESK" "loginctl show-user"
check "desktop: through the user manager" has "$DESK" "systemd-run --user"
check "desktop: the unit is collected" has "$DESK" "--collect"
check "desktop: its output into the job log" has "$DESK" "StandardOutput=append:"
check "desktop: session check before the start" before "$DESK" "loginctl show-user" "systemd-run --user"
check "desktop: never sudo" lacks "$DESK" "sudo"

check "put: refuses a name with .." bash -c "! LINVPN_SSH=a@b LINVPN_REMOTE_PRINT=1 '$SCRIPT' put /tmp ../x >/dev/null 2>&1"
check "fetch: refuses a path with .." bash -c "! LINVPN_SSH=a@b LINVPN_REMOTE_PRINT=1 '$SCRIPT' fetch ../etc /tmp/x >/dev/null 2>&1"
check "attach: refuses a bad job id" bash -c "! LINVPN_SSH=a@b LINVPN_REMOTE_PRINT=1 '$SCRIPT' attach 'j1;rm' >/dev/null 2>&1"

check "no address in the script" bash -c "! grep -En '([0-9]{1,3}\.){3}[0-9]{1,3}' '$SCRIPT'"

echo "$PASS passed, $FAIL failed"
[ "$FAIL" -eq 0 ]
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `scripts/lin-remote.sh`**

```bash
#!/bin/bash
# Runs the working tree on the Linux test VM over SSH (docs/architecture.md, "The Linux test machine").
#
#   scripts/lin-remote.sh sync                         copy HEAD + uncommitted changes of tracked files there
#   scripts/lin-remote.sh run 'COMMAND'                sync, then COMMAND in the remote checkout (a logged job)
#   scripts/lin-remote.sh run-nosync 'COMMAND'         COMMAND in the remote checkout as it is (no sync)
#   scripts/lin-remote.sh desktop 'COMMAND'            sync, then COMMAND in linvpn-dev's desktop session
#   scripts/lin-remote.sh test [EXTRA CARGO ARGS]      sync, then the CI linux job's test set
#   scripts/lin-remote.sh ignored                      sync, then list the ignored tests
#   scripts/lin-remote.sh attach JOB                   follow a job started earlier (its log, then its exit code)
#   scripts/lin-remote.sh put LOCAL NAME               copy a local file or folder to <checkout>/.linvpn-put/NAME
#   scripts/lin-remote.sh fetch REMOTE_PATH LOCAL_DIR  copy a build product back (relative to the checkout)
#
# Environment: LINVPN_SSH (ssh destination linvpn-dev@host, required), LINVPN_REMOTE_DIR (default "linvpn-work",
# relative to the remote home; one per concurrent agent), LINVPN_JOB_WAIT (seconds a job may take, default 5400),
# LINVPN_REMOTE_PRINT=1 prints the remote scripts instead of running them (scripts/test-lin-remote.sh).
#
# Every command runs as a job: a script in <checkout>/.lin-jobs/<id>.sh, started detached (setsid) with its output in
# <id>.log and its exit code in <id>.rc; this script polls the log over short SSH calls, so a hanging SSH session
# never loses the command or its result (`attach <id>` picks it up again). `test` and `run` run with CI=1 under a
# login shell (rustup's ~/.cargo/env); `test` wraps cargo in dbus-run-session (a private session bus: no test sees
# the desktop's keyring or notifications). `desktop` checks that linvpn-dev is logged in at the VM's desktop and starts
# the job with `systemd-run --user` in the user's systemd instance, which has the desktop's WAYLAND_DISPLAY, DISPLAY and
# session bus, and which polkit counts as the graphical session. The remote account has no administrator rights;
# nothing here needs any. Nothing secret is sent: the bundle holds only the repository's tracked content.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."

SSH_DEST="${LINVPN_SSH:-}"
SSH_OPTS=(-o BatchMode=yes -o IdentitiesOnly=yes -o LogLevel=ERROR -o ConnectTimeout=20
  -o ServerAliveInterval=15 -o ServerAliveCountMax=4 -i "$HOME/.ssh/linvpn_dev"
  -o UserKnownHostsFile="$HOME/.ssh/linvpn_known_hosts" -o StrictHostKeyChecking=yes)
REMOTE_DIR="${LINVPN_REMOTE_DIR:-linvpn-work}"
WAIT="${LINVPN_JOB_WAIT:-5400}"
PRINT="${LINVPN_REMOTE_PRINT:-}"
# Refs are shared by every worktree of the repository: one ref per run.
REF="refs/linvpn/sync-$$"

die() { echo "error: $*" >&2; exit 1; }

[ $# -ge 1 ] || { sed -n '4,12p' "$0" | sed 's/^# \{0,1\}//'; exit 1; }
[ -n "$SSH_DEST" ] || die "set LINVPN_SSH=linvpn-dev@<VM address>"
[[ "$REMOTE_DIR" =~ ^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+(/[A-Za-z0-9._-]+)*$ ]] || die "LINVPN_REMOTE_DIR: letters, digits, . _ - and /"
[[ "$WAIT" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || die "LINVPN_JOB_WAIT: seconds"

# The CI linux job's packages; a package whose crate does not exist yet is left out.
PACKAGES=()
for pkg_dir in macvpn-core:crates/core macvpn-app-core:crates/app-core macvpn-platform-api:crates/platform-api \
  macvpn-platform-linux:crates/platform-linux macvpn-unixsys:crates/unixsys macvpn-helper:crates/helper \
  macvpn-control-client:examples/macvpn-control-client macvpn-app:app/src-tauri; do
  if [ -f "${pkg_dir#*:}/Cargo.toml" ]; then PACKAGES+=(-p "${pkg_dir%%:*}"); fi
done

WORK="$(mktemp -d "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/lin-remote.XXXXXXXX")"
cleanup() { rm -rf "${WORK:?}"; git update-ref -d "$REF" 2>/dev/null || true; }
trap cleanup EXIT

# One short SSH call: the script text goes base64-encoded (no re-quoting), runs in bash with stdin from /dev/null.
remote() {
  local b64
  if [ -n "$PRINT" ]; then
    printf '%s\n' "$1"
    return
  fi
  b64="$(printf '%s\n' "$1" | base64 | tr -d '\n')"
  # shellcheck disable=SC2029 # expanded locally on purpose: the base64 text is the whole command
  ssh -n "${SSH_OPTS[@]}" "$SSH_DEST" "echo $b64 | base64 -d | bash -s </dev/null"
}

sync() {
  local rev tree
  # A snapshot commit of the tracked files as they are on disk, made in a copy of the index (scripts/win-remote.sh).
  cp "$(git rev-parse --git-path index)" "$WORK/index"
  tree="$(GIT_INDEX_FILE="$WORK/index" git add -u && GIT_INDEX_FILE="$WORK/index" git write-tree)" \
    || die "could not snapshot the working tree"
  if [ "$tree" = "$(git rev-parse 'HEAD^{tree}')" ]; then
    rev="$(git rev-parse HEAD)"
  else
    rev="$(git commit-tree --no-gpg-sign "$tree" -p HEAD -m "lin-remote: uncommitted changes on $(git rev-parse --short HEAD)")"
  fi
  git update-ref "$REF" "$rev"
  git bundle create "$WORK/sync.bundle" "$REF" >/dev/null 2>&1 || die "git bundle failed"
  if [ -n "$PRINT" ]; then
    echo "(print only: nothing is synced)"
    return
  fi
  remote "mkdir -p ~/'$REMOTE_DIR' && cd ~/'$REMOTE_DIR' && { [ -d .git ] || git init -q; }"
  scp -q "${SSH_OPTS[@]}" "$WORK/sync.bundle" "$SSH_DEST:$REMOTE_DIR/.sync.bundle"
  remote "set -e; cd ~/'$REMOTE_DIR'; git fetch -q .sync.bundle '$REF'; git checkout -q --force --detach FETCH_HEAD
git clean -q -fdx -e /target -e node_modules -e /dist -e /.lin-jobs -e /.linvpn-put -e '/spikes/*/target'
rm -f .sync.bundle; git log -1 --format='synced %h %s'"
}

# The job script: the command in the checkout, its exit code written last (atomically: .rc.tmp, then mv).
job_script() {
  local id="$1" command="$2"
  cat <<SCRIPT
#!/bin/bash
set -o pipefail
cd "\$HOME/$REMOTE_DIR" || { echo 97 > ".lin-jobs/$id.rc"; exit 97; }
export CI=1
{
$command
}
rc=\$?
echo "\$rc" > "\$HOME/$REMOTE_DIR/.lin-jobs/$id.rc.tmp" && mv "\$HOME/$REMOTE_DIR/.lin-jobs/$id.rc.tmp" "\$HOME/$REMOTE_DIR/.lin-jobs/$id.rc"
SCRIPT
}

new_id() { echo "j$(date +%Y%m%d%H%M%S)-$$"; }

# Writes the job script remotely and starts it detached in the SSH user's own session (`plain`) or, after the
# desktop-session check, in the user manager (`desktop`).
start_job() {
  local mode="$1" id="$2" command="$3" b64 launch
  b64="$(job_script "$id" "$command" | base64 | tr -d '\n')"
  if [ "$mode" = desktop ]; then
    launch="session=\$(loginctl show-user \"\$USER\" -p Display --value 2>/dev/null)
type=\$(loginctl show-session \"\$session\" -p Type --value 2>/dev/null)
case \"\$type\" in wayland|x11) ;; *) echo 'no desktop session of '\"\$USER\"': log in as '\"\$USER\"' at the VM desktop (it may be locked), then run again' >&2; exit 3 ;; esac
systemd-run --user --quiet --collect --unit='linvpn-$id' -p StandardOutput=append:\"\$HOME/$REMOTE_DIR/.lin-jobs/$id.log\" -p StandardError=append:\"\$HOME/$REMOTE_DIR/.lin-jobs/$id.log\" /bin/bash -l \"\$HOME/$REMOTE_DIR/.lin-jobs/$id.sh\""
  else
    launch="setsid --fork /bin/bash -l \"\$HOME/$REMOTE_DIR/.lin-jobs/$id.sh\" > \"\$HOME/$REMOTE_DIR/.lin-jobs/$id.log\" 2>&1 < /dev/null"
  fi
  remote "set -e; mkdir -p ~/'$REMOTE_DIR'/.lin-jobs; cd ~/'$REMOTE_DIR'/.lin-jobs
rm -f '$id.log' '$id.rc' '$id.rc.tmp'; : > '$id.log'
echo $b64 | base64 -d > '$id.sh'
$launch
echo 'job $id started (scripts/lin-remote.sh attach $id)' >&2"
  [ -n "$PRINT" ] && job_script "$id" "$command"
  return 0
}

# Polls the job's log (new bytes only) and its exit code; a failed poll is retried, 12 in a row end the wait.
follow() {
  local id="$1" offset=0 waited=0 misses=0 rc n
  [ -n "$PRINT" ] && return 0
  while :; do
    if remote "tail -c +$((offset + 1)) ~/'$REMOTE_DIR'/.lin-jobs/'$id'.log 2>/dev/null; true" > "$WORK/chunk"; then
      misses=0
      n=$(wc -c < "$WORK/chunk" | tr -d ' ')
      offset=$((offset + n))
      cat "$WORK/chunk"
    else
      misses=$((misses + 1))
      [ "$misses" -lt 12 ] || die "lost the VM while following job $id (scripts/lin-remote.sh attach $id)"
    fi
    rc="$(remote "cat ~/'$REMOTE_DIR'/.lin-jobs/'$id'.rc 2>/dev/null; true" 2>/dev/null || true)"
    if [[ "$rc" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]]; then
      remote "tail -c +$((offset + 1)) ~/'$REMOTE_DIR'/.lin-jobs/'$id'.log 2>/dev/null; true" || true
      return "$rc"
    fi
    [ "$waited" -lt "$WAIT" ] || die "job $id: no exit code after $WAIT s (scripts/lin-remote.sh attach $id)"
    sleep 5
    waited=$((waited + 5))
  done
}

job() {
  local mode="$1" command="$2" id
  id="$(new_id)"
  start_job "$mode" "$id" "$command"
  follow "$id"
}

case "$1" in
  sync) sync ;;
  run)
    [ $# -eq 2 ] || die "run needs exactly one quoted command"
    sync
    job plain "$2"
    ;;
  run-nosync)
    [ $# -eq 2 ] || die "run-nosync needs exactly one quoted command"
    job plain "$2"
    ;;
  desktop)
    [ $# -eq 2 ] || die "desktop needs exactly one quoted command"
    sync
    job desktop "$2"
    ;;
  test)
    shift
    sync
    job plain "dbus-run-session -- cargo test --locked ${PACKAGES[*]} --lib --tests --no-fail-fast $*"
    ;;
  ignored)
    sync
    job plain "cargo test --locked ${PACKAGES[*]} --lib --tests -- --list --ignored 2>/dev/null | grep ': test\$' | sort"
    ;;
  attach)
    [ $# -eq 2 ] && [[ "$2" =~ ^j[0-9]{14}-[0-9]+$ ]] || die "attach needs a job id (j<timestamp>-<pid>)"
    follow "$2"
    ;;
  put)
    [ $# -eq 3 ] || die "put needs LOCAL NAME"
    [[ "$3" =~ ^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+(/[A-Za-z0-9._-]+)*$ && ! "$3" =~ (^|/)\.\.(/|$) ]] || die "NAME: letters, digits, . _ - and /, no .."
    [ -e "$2" ] || die "not found: $2"
    if [ -n "$PRINT" ]; then echo "scp -r $2 -> $REMOTE_DIR/.linvpn-put/$3"; exit 0; fi
    remote "mkdir -p ~/'$REMOTE_DIR'/.linvpn-put/'$(dirname "$3")'"
    scp -q -r "${SSH_OPTS[@]}" "$2" "$SSH_DEST:$REMOTE_DIR/.linvpn-put/$3"
    ;;
  fetch)
    [ $# -eq 3 ] || die "fetch needs REMOTE_PATH LOCAL_DIR"
    [[ "$2" =~ ^[A-Za-z0-9._/-]+$ && ! "$2" =~ (^|/)\.\.(/|$) ]] || die "REMOTE_PATH: letters, digits, . _ / - only, no .."
    if [ -n "$PRINT" ]; then echo "scp -r $REMOTE_DIR/$2 -> $3"; exit 0; fi
    mkdir -p "$3"
    scp -q -r "${SSH_OPTS[@]}" "$SSH_DEST:$REMOTE_DIR/$2" "$3/"
    ;;
  *) die "unknown command: $1" ;;
esac
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Document it in `docs/architecture.md`**

Add after the section "Checking Windows":

```markdown
## Checking Linux

### The Linux test machine

The coordinator reaches an Ubuntu 26.04 desktop VM (GNOME, Wayland) over SSH as the **non-admin** user `linvpn-dev`
(key `~/.ssh/linvpn_dev` on the Mac, host key pinned in `~/.ssh/linvpn_known_hosts`, no alias in `~/.ssh/config`;
the address is never in the repository: `LINVPN_SSH=linvpn-dev@<VM address>`). The maintainer installed the build
dependencies and the NetworkManager VPN plugins with apt (LinVPN plan, CP0); rustup lives in the user's home.
Anything that needs root (apt, installing the `.deb`, a polkit password) is the maintainer's, at a checkpoint.

`scripts/lin-remote.sh` syncs the working tree (HEAD plus uncommitted changes of tracked files; `git add -N` a new
file first) and runs every command as a **job**: detached on the VM, its output in `<checkout>/.lin-jobs/<id>.log`,
polled from the Mac over short SSH calls, so a hanging SSH session loses nothing (`attach <id>` continues):

    scripts/lin-remote.sh test                      # the CI linux job's test set (dbus-run-session, CI=1)
    scripts/lin-remote.sh run 'cargo test --locked -p macvpn-platform-linux'
    scripts/lin-remote.sh desktop 'cargo test --locked -p macvpn-platform-linux -- --ignored desktop'
    scripts/lin-remote.sh run 'scripts/build-deb.sh'
    scripts/lin-remote.sh fetch dist/linux ./dist/linux

`desktop` runs the job with `systemd-run --user` in the user's systemd instance, which has the desktop session's
display and session bus and which polkit counts as the graphical session; `linvpn-dev` must be logged in at the VM's
desktop (the screen may be locked). Several worktrees use separate remote checkouts:
`LINVPN_REMOTE_DIR=linvpn-work-slot<N>` (warm build directories, reused, never deleted after a task).
```

`spikes/l0-2-tray/src/main.rs`:

```rust
//! LinVPN L0-2 spike: two tray icons side by side — Tauri's (`T`) and an own StatusNotifierItem over ksni (`S`) —
//! and one undecorated panel window. Every event is logged with a timestamp to stderr. Left click: S calls
//! `activate(x, y)`; T delivers whatever Tauri delivers on Linux. Either one shows the panel at the top-right corner
//! of the work area of the current monitor, then logs where the window really is and whether it has the focus.
//! The panel hides on focus loss (logged). Throwaway code.

use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};
use tauri::menu::{Menu, MenuItem};
use tauri::tray::TrayIconBuilder;
use tauri::{AppHandle, Manager, PhysicalPosition, WindowEvent};

fn log(what: &str) {
    let t = SystemTime::now().duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH).unwrap().as_millis();
    eprintln!("L0-2 {t} {what}");
}

fn show_panel(app: &AppHandle, source: &str) {
    let Some(panel) = app.get_webview_window("panel") else { return };
    let monitor = panel.current_monitor().ok().flatten().or_else(|| panel.primary_monitor().ok().flatten());
    if let Some(m) = monitor {
        let area = m.work_area();
        let size = panel.outer_size().unwrap();
        let x = area.position.x + area.size.width as i32 - size.width as i32 - 8;
        let y = area.position.y + 8;
        log(&format!("{source}: work area {:?} {:?}, placing at {x},{y}", area.position, area.size));
        let _ = panel.set_position(PhysicalPosition::new(x, y));
    }
    let _ = panel.show();
    let _ = panel.set_focus();
    let p = panel.clone();
    std::thread::spawn(move || {
        std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_millis(400));
        log(&format!(
            "after show: outer_position {:?}, focused {:?}, visible {:?}",
            p.outer_position(),
            p.is_focused(),
            p.is_visible()
        ));
    });
}

struct Sni {
    app: AppHandle,
    icon: ksni::Icon,
}

impl ksni::Tray for Sni {
    // ksni API: ItemIsMenu = false, so a host may send Activate on a left click.
    const MENU_ON_ACTIVATE: bool = false;

    fn id(&self) -> String {
        "local.linvpn.spike.tray".into()
    }
    fn title(&self) -> String {
        "L0-2 S".into()
    }
    fn icon_pixmap(&self) -> Vec<ksni::Icon> {
        vec![self.icon.clone()]
    }
    fn activate(&mut self, x: i32, y: i32) {
        log(&format!("S activate x={x} y={y}"));
        let app = self.app.clone();
        let _ = self.app.run_on_main_thread(move || show_panel(&app, "S"));
    }
    fn secondary_activate(&mut self, x: i32, y: i32) {
        log(&format!("S secondary_activate x={x} y={y}"));
    }
    fn menu(&self) -> Vec<ksni::MenuItem<Self>> {
        vec![
            ksni::menu::StandardItem {
                label: "Open panel (S)".into(),
                activate: Box::new(|t: &mut Sni| {
                    log("S menu: open panel");
                    let app = t.app.clone();
                    let _ = t.app.run_on_main_thread(move || show_panel(&app, "S-menu"));
                }),
                ..Default::default()
            }
            .into(),
        ]
    }
}

/// RGBA → ARGB32 (big-endian), the SNI pixmap format.
fn argb(rgba: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8> {
    rgba.chunks_exact(4).flat_map(|p| [p[3], p[0], p[1], p[2]]).collect()
}

fn main() {
    tauri::Builder::default()
        .setup(|app| {
            log(&format!(
                "start: XDG_SESSION_TYPE={:?} GDK_BACKEND={:?} WAYLAND_DISPLAY={:?}",
                std::env::var("XDG_SESSION_TYPE").ok(),
                std::env::var("GDK_BACKEND").ok(),
                std::env::var("WAYLAND_DISPLAY").ok()
            ));
            let image = tauri::image::Image::from_bytes(include_bytes!("../icon.png"))?;
            // T: Tauri's tray with a menu.
            let open = MenuItem::with_id(app, "open", "Open panel (T)", true, None::<&str>)?;
            let menu = Menu::with_items(app, &[&open])?;
            TrayIconBuilder::with_id("t")
                .icon(image.clone())
                .menu(&menu)
                .show_menu_on_left_click(false)
                .on_tray_icon_event(|_, e| log(&format!("T event {e:?}")))
                .on_menu_event(|app, e| {
                    log(&format!("T menu {:?}", e.id()));
                    show_panel(app, "T-menu");
                })
                .build(app)?;
            // S: an own SNI over ksni.
            let icon = ksni::Icon { width: image.width() as i32, height: image.height() as i32, data: argb(image.rgba()) };
            // ksni API: spawn the service (blocking flavour).
            let _handle = ksni::blocking::TrayMethods::spawn(Sni { app: app.handle().clone(), icon })
                .map_err(|e| format!("ksni: {e}"))?;
            std::mem::forget(_handle);
            let panel = app.get_webview_window("panel").unwrap();
            panel.on_window_event(|e| {
                if let WindowEvent::Focused(f) = e {
                    log(&format!("panel focused={f}"));
                }
            });
            let p = panel.clone();
            panel.on_window_event(move |e| {
                if let WindowEvent::Focused(false) = e {
                    let _ = p.hide();
                    log("panel hidden on blur");
                }
            });
            Ok(())
        })
        .run(tauri::generate_context!())
        .expect("tauri");
}
```

## Task 5: spike L0-3 — WireGuard's last handshake without root — **done**

Done by the coordinator on 2026-10-29 (commit `fcc891b`): `docs/superpowers/spikes/2026-10-29-linvpn-wireguard-liveness.md`. The handshake time is not readable without root (NM's D-Bus has none; `wg` and generic netlink get `EPERM`); LinVPN uses the counter rule on `/sys/class/net/<iface>/statistics` (dead when tx grows while rx stays still for about 200 s). The spec says so since `ee9d4e0`. L-b implements it; L-a needs nothing from it.

---

## Task 6: spike L0-4 — OpenVPN's static and dynamic (CRV1) challenge through an NM secret agent

**Status: deferred** until the maintainer starts the VPN lab (CP-lab). No L-a task depends on it; it feeds the L-b plan only. When it runs, follow spike L0-1's polkit rule: the connection gets `permissions = user:linvpn-dev` **before** it is added, it is added and activated with `AddAndActivateConnection2` (`persist = volatile`, `bind-activation = dbus-client`) from the spike agent itself, never with `nmcli connection import` (which asks for the admin password), and only after a non-interactive `CheckAuthorization`. The steps below are written that way.

**Goal:** prove that a LinVPN-owned NM secret agent receives OpenVPN's challenges (the static challenge before the connect, CRV1 during it), what NM sends as hints, which secret keys answer them, and whether GNOME Shell's own agent interferes. Uses the VPN lab's `openvpn-static-challenge.ovpn` and, for CRV1, `openvpn-tun-dco.ovpn` with the user `labotp`.

**Files:**
- Create: `spikes/l0-4-secret-agent/Cargo.toml`, `spikes/l0-4-secret-agent/src/main.rs` (a standalone project outside the workspace)
- Create: `docs/superpowers/spikes/2026-10-29-linvpn-secret-agent.md`

- [ ] **Step 1: The `zbus` version**

Use the version Task 9 pinned in `crates/platform-linux/Cargo.toml` (if Task 9 is not merged yet: `cargo search zbus --limit 1`), exactly, and record it in the findings.

- [ ] **Step 2: The spike agent** (it adds and activates its own volatile connection: spike L0-1's polkit rule)

`spikes/l0-4-secret-agent/src/main.rs`:

```rust
//! LinVPN L0-4 spike: an NM secret agent (`org.freedesktop.NetworkManager.SecretAgent`) that logs every request
//! with its hints and answers the OpenVPN plugin from environment variables, then activates the connection itself
//! (so NM asks this agent, the activating process's, first). Usage (after Step 4's change):
//!   l0-4-secret-agent <remote> <port> <udp|tcp> <ca.pem> <tls-crypt key|-> <static-challenge text|->
//! Env: L04_USER, L04_PASSWORD, L04_CODE (the lab's one-time code). Secrets are never printed: only their key names.
//! Throwaway code.

use std::collections::HashMap;
use zbus::blocking::{Connection, Proxy};
use zbus::zvariant::{OwnedObjectPath, OwnedValue, Value};

type Settings = HashMap<String, HashMap<String, OwnedValue>>;

/// The run is the static-challenge profile (answer the password as `SCRV1:b64(password):b64(code)`).
static STATIC_CHALLENGE: std::sync::atomic::AtomicBool = std::sync::atomic::AtomicBool::new(false);

/// Standard base64 with padding (a spike needs no crate for it).
fn b64(input: &[u8]) -> String {
    const T: &[u8; 64] = b"ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz0123456789+/";
    let mut out = String::new();
    for chunk in input.chunks(3) {
        let b = [chunk[0], *chunk.get(1).unwrap_or(&0), *chunk.get(2).unwrap_or(&0)];
        let n = (u32::from(b[0]) << 16) | (u32::from(b[1]) << 8) | u32::from(b[2]);
        for i in 0..4 {
            if i <= chunk.len() {
                out.push(T[((n >> (18 - 6 * i)) & 63) as usize] as char);
            } else {
                out.push('=');
            }
        }
    }
    out
}

struct Agent;

#[zbus::interface(name = "org.freedesktop.NetworkManager.SecretAgent")]
impl Agent {
    fn get_secrets(
        &self,
        connection: Settings,
        connection_path: OwnedObjectPath,
        setting_name: String,
        hints: Vec<String>,
        flags: u32,
    ) -> zbus::fdo::Result<HashMap<String, HashMap<String, OwnedValue>>> {
        eprintln!("L0-4 GetSecrets path={connection_path} setting={setting_name} flags={flags:#x} hints={hints:?}");
        if let Some(vpn) = connection.get("vpn") {
            let keys: Vec<&String> = vpn.keys().collect();
            eprintln!("L0-4 vpn setting keys: {keys:?}");
            if let Some(data) = vpn.get("data") {
                eprintln!("L0-4 vpn.data: {data:?}");
            }
        }
        let env = |k: &str| std::env::var(k).unwrap_or_default();
        let mut secrets: HashMap<String, String> = HashMap::new();
        let password = if STATIC_CHALLENGE.load(std::sync::atomic::Ordering::SeqCst) {
            format!("SCRV1:{}:{}", b64(env("L04_PASSWORD").as_bytes()), b64(env("L04_CODE").as_bytes()))
        } else {
            env("L04_PASSWORD")
        };
        secrets.insert("password".into(), password);
        // The challenge answer: the hint names decide which key the plugin reads; answer every plausible one and
        // log which one the plugin used (its log in the journal says).
        let code = env("L04_CODE");
        for h in &hints {
            if let Some(rest) = h.strip_prefix("x-dynamic-challenge") {
                eprintln!("L0-4 dynamic challenge hint{rest}");
            }
            if h.starts_with("x-vpn-message:") {
                eprintln!("L0-4 message hint: {h}");
            }
        }
        // CRV1: the plugin asks for `challenge-response` with an `x-dynamic-challenge…:challenge-response` hint.
        secrets.insert("challenge-response".into(), code.clone());
        let secrets: HashMap<String, OwnedValue> =
            [("secrets".to_string(), OwnedValue::try_from(Value::from(secrets)).unwrap())].into();
        eprintln!("L0-4 answering with keys password, challenge-response");
        Ok([("vpn".to_string(), secrets)].into())
    }

    fn cancel_get_secrets(&self, connection_path: OwnedObjectPath, setting_name: String) {
        eprintln!("L0-4 CancelGetSecrets {connection_path} {setting_name}");
    }

    fn save_secrets(&self, _connection: Settings, connection_path: OwnedObjectPath) {
        eprintln!("L0-4 SaveSecrets {connection_path} (ignored)");
    }

    fn delete_secrets(&self, _connection: Settings, connection_path: OwnedObjectPath) {
        eprintln!("L0-4 DeleteSecrets {connection_path} (ignored)");
    }
}

fn main() -> zbus::Result<()> {
    // Usage: l0-4-secret-agent <remote host> <port> <udp|tcp> <ca.pem> <tls-crypt key file or -> <static-challenge text or ->
    let args: Vec<String> = std::env::args().skip(1).collect();
    let [remote, port, proto, ca, tls_crypt, challenge] = args.as_slice() else {
        panic!("usage: l0-4-secret-agent <remote> <port> <udp|tcp> <ca.pem> <tls-crypt|-> <static-challenge|->")
    };
    let bus = Connection::system()?;
    // Spike L0-1's rule: polkit first, non-interactively (flags 0 never shows a dialog).
    let authority = Proxy::new(
        &bus,
        "org.freedesktop.PolicyKit1",
        "/org/freedesktop/PolicyKit1/Authority",
        "org.freedesktop.PolicyKit1.Authority",
    )?;
    let subject: (&str, HashMap<&str, Value>) =
        ("unix-process", HashMap::from([("pid", Value::from(std::process::id())), ("start-time", Value::from(0u64))]));
    for action in ["org.freedesktop.NetworkManager.settings.modify.own", "org.freedesktop.NetworkManager.network-control"] {
        let (authorized, _challenge, _details): (bool, bool, HashMap<String, String>) =
            authority.call("CheckAuthorization", &(&subject, action, HashMap::<&str, &str>::new(), 0u32, ""))?;
        if !authorized {
            eprintln!("L0-4 not authorized for {action} without a prompt: run it in the desktop session");
            return Ok(());
        }
    }
    bus.object_server().at("/org/freedesktop/NetworkManager/SecretAgent", Agent)?;
    let manager = Proxy::new(
        &bus,
        "org.freedesktop.NetworkManager",
        "/org/freedesktop/NetworkManager/AgentManager",
        "org.freedesktop.NetworkManager.AgentManager",
    )?;
    // Capabilities: 1 = VPN hints (NM sends the plugin's hints to the agent).
    manager.call_method("RegisterWithCapabilities", &("local.linvpn.spike.agent", 1u32))?;
    eprintln!("L0-4 agent registered as {}", bus.unique_name().map(|n| n.to_string()).unwrap_or_default());

    let user = std::env::var("USER").unwrap_or_default();
    let mut data: HashMap<String, String> = HashMap::from([
        ("remote".into(), remote.clone()),
        ("port".into(), port.clone()),
        ("proto-tcp".into(), if proto == "tcp" { "yes".into() } else { "no".into() }),
        ("dev-type".into(), "tun".into()),
        ("connection-type".into(), "password".into()),
        ("ca".into(), ca.clone()),
        ("password-flags".into(), "1".into()),
        ("challenge-response-flags".into(), "1".into()),
        ("username".into(), std::env::var("L04_USER").unwrap_or_default()),
    ]);
    if tls_crypt != "-" {
        data.insert("tls-crypt".into(), tls_crypt.clone());
    }
    // NM-openvpn has no static-challenge key (L0-1 findings, "MFA"): the agent answers the password secret in
    // OpenVPN's SCRV1 form itself.
    STATIC_CHALLENGE.store(challenge != "-", std::sync::atomic::Ordering::SeqCst);
    let uuid = format!("{:032x}", std::time::SystemTime::now().duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH).unwrap().as_nanos());
    let uuid = format!("{}-{}-{}-{}-{}", &uuid[0..8], &uuid[8..12], &uuid[12..16], &uuid[16..20], &uuid[20..32]);
    let settings: HashMap<&str, HashMap<&str, Value>> = HashMap::from([
        (
            "connection",
            HashMap::from([
                ("id", Value::from("l0-4-spike")),
                ("uuid", Value::from(uuid.as_str())),
                ("type", Value::from("vpn")),
                ("autoconnect", Value::from(false)),
                ("permissions", Value::from(vec![format!("user:{user}")])),
            ]),
        ),
        (
            "vpn",
            HashMap::from([
                ("service-type", Value::from("org.freedesktop.NetworkManager.openvpn")),
                ("user-name", Value::from(std::env::var("L04_USER").unwrap_or_default())),
                ("data", Value::from(data)),
            ]),
        ),
        ("ipv4", HashMap::from([("method", Value::from("auto")), ("never-default", Value::from(true))])),
        ("ipv6", HashMap::from([("method", Value::from("ignore"))])),
    ]);
    let nm = Proxy::new(
        &bus,
        "org.freedesktop.NetworkManager",
        "/org/freedesktop/NetworkManager",
        "org.freedesktop.NetworkManager",
    )?;
    let root = zbus::zvariant::ObjectPath::try_from("/")?;
    let options: HashMap<&str, Value> =
        HashMap::from([("persist", Value::from("volatile")), ("bind-activation", Value::from("dbus-client"))]);
    let (path, active, _result): (OwnedObjectPath, OwnedObjectPath, HashMap<String, OwnedValue>) =
        nm.call("AddAndActivateConnection2", &(settings, &root, &root, options))?;
    eprintln!("L0-4 added {path}, activating {active}");
    std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_secs(90));
    eprintln!("L0-4 done (the connection goes away with this process: volatile, bound to the D-Bus client)");
    Ok(())
}
```

Run: `LINVPN_REMOTE_DIR=linvpn-work-slot1 scripts/lin-remote.sh run 'cd spikes/l0-4-secret-agent && cargo build 2>&1 | tail -n 20'`
Expected: `Finished`. Fix compile errors against the pinned zbus's docs; note API differences in the findings.

- [ ] **Step 4: The static challenge (the agent adds and activates the connection itself; no `nmcli connection import`)**

The profile's values come from the `.ovpn` on the VM: its `remote` line and its inline `<ca>` and `<tls-crypt>` blocks, written to `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/l0-4/` with mode 0600 and passed as file paths. The agent checks polkit first without a dialog, then calls `AddAndActivateConnection2` with `permissions = user:linvpn-dev`, `persist = volatile`, `bind-activation = dbus-client` and a split tunnel (`never-default`; the lab pushes its routes).

Run (desktop context; the agent reads the values from the credentials file on the VM, so they never pass the Mac's command line):

```bash
LINVPN_REMOTE_DIR=linvpn-work-slot1 scripts/lin-remote.sh desktop 'set -e
C=.linvpn-put/lab/CREDENTIALS.txt
export L04_USER=labotp
export L04_PASSWORD="$(sed -n "s/^Password: *//p" $C)"
export L04_CODE="$(sed -n "s/.*code \([0-9]*\);.*/\1/p" $C)"
P=.linvpn-put/lab/openvpn-static-challenge.ovpn
umask 077; R="$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/l0-4"; mkdir -p "$R"
sed -n "/<ca>/,/<\/ca>/p" $P | sed "1d;\$d" > "$R/ca.pem"
sed -n "/<tls-crypt>/,/<\/tls-crypt>/p" $P | sed "1d;\$d" > "$R/tc.key"
set -- $(sed -n "s/^remote //p" $P | head -n 1)
CH="$(sed -n "s/^static-challenge \"\(.*\)\" [01]$/\1/p" $P)"
spikes/l0-4-secret-agent/target/debug/l0-4-secret-agent "$1" "$2" "${3:-udp}" "$R/ca.pem" "$([ -s "$R/tc.key" ] && echo "$R/tc.key" || echo -)" "${CH:--}" 2> target/l0-4-static.log || true
rm -rf "$R"
journalctl --since "-3 min" -u NetworkManager --no-pager | grep -i -E "openvpn|vpn" > target/l0-4-static-nm.txt || true'
```

Expected: `target/l0-4-static.log` on the VM shows `GetSecrets` with the hints and whether the connection reached `activated`; the connection is gone afterwards (volatile, and bound to the agent's process). (A non-admin user may read NetworkManager's journal lines only in the `systemd-journal` group; if `target/l0-4-static-nm.txt` is empty, say so in the findings: that is why L-b has `linvpn-logd`.)

- [ ] **Step 5: The dynamic challenge (CRV1)**

The same command with `P=.linvpn-put/lab/openvpn-tun-dco.ovpn` (no static challenge: the last argument becomes `-`) and the log names `l0-4-crv1*`. Expected: a second `GetSecrets` during the connection with a dynamic-challenge hint (the CRV1 text), or the connection failing: record which.

- [ ] **Step 6: Fetch the logs and write the findings**

```bash
for f in l0-4-static.log l0-4-static-nm.txt l0-4-crv1.log l0-4-crv1-nm.txt; do
  LINVPN_REMOTE_DIR=linvpn-work-slot1 scripts/lin-remote.sh fetch "target/$f" target/l0-4
done
grep -niE 'password=|L04_' target/l0-4/* || echo "no secret in the logs"
```

Expected: `no secret in the logs`.

`docs/superpowers/spikes/2026-10-29-linvpn-secret-agent.md`:

```markdown
# LinVPN L0-4 — OpenVPN's challenges through an NM secret agent

**Date:** <date> · **VM:** Ubuntu 26.04, NM <version>, network-manager-openvpn <version>, zbus <5.x.y>

## Static challenge (`openvpn-static-challenge.ovpn`, user labotp)
- GetSecrets calls (count, setting, flags, hints): <…>
- The keys the plugin read (`password` in the SCRV1 form, `challenge-response`, other): <…>
- Result: activated / failed (<reason>)

## Dynamic challenge (CRV1, `openvpn-tun-dco.ovpn`, user labotp)
- GetSecrets calls during the connection (hints with the challenge text?): <…>
- Answer accepted: yes / no
- Timing: NM's wait for the agent (seconds)

## Agent order
Did GNOME Shell's own agent prompt at any time? <…> Does NM ask the activating process's agent first? <…>

## NM's journal as linvpn-dev
Readable: yes / no (`systemd-journal` group). <consequence for `linvpn-logd`>

## Decision for L-b
<the secret-agent design: capabilities, the hint names to parse, the keys to answer, what the code prompt shows; or
```
