- [ ] **Step 2: The pure code** — `pure/instance.rs`:

```rust
//! The single-instance name on the session bus: `local.linvpn.Instance`; a sandbox owns
//! `local.linvpn.Sandbox.h<16 hex digits of SHA-256(the sandbox path)>`, so a smoke run never meets an installed
//! LinVPN. And the files of a launch: its absolute arguments (`linvpn %F`); options and relative paths are not files.

use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};
use std::ffi::OsString;
use std::os::unix::ffi::OsStrExt;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

pub fn bus_name(sandbox: Option<&Path>) -> String {
    match sandbox {
        None => "local.linvpn.Instance".into(),
        Some(p) => {
            let digest = Sha256::digest(p.as_os_str().as_bytes());
            let hex: String = digest.iter().take(8).map(|b| format!("{b:02x}")).collect();
            format!("local.linvpn.Sandbox.h{hex}")
        }
    }
}

pub fn launch_files(args: impl IntoIterator<Item = OsString>) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    args.into_iter().map(PathBuf::from).filter(|p| p.is_absolute()).collect()
}
```

`pure/autostart.rs`:

```rust
//! Launch at login: `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/autostart/linvpn.desktop` (the XDG Autostart spec; GNOME reads
//! `X-GNOME-Autostart-enabled`). On: our file exists and neither `Hidden=true` nor `X-GNOME-Autostart-enabled=false`
//! is in its `[Desktop Entry]` group. A file another tool turned off counts as off, with a note.

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum State {
    On,
    Off,
    TurnedOffElsewhere,
}

pub fn desktop_file() -> String {
    "[Desktop Entry]\nType=Application\nName=LinVPN\nExec=/usr/bin/linvpn\nIcon=linvpn\nTerminal=false\nNoDisplay=true\nX-GNOME-Autostart-enabled=true\n".to_string()
}

pub fn state_of(text: Option<&str>) -> State {
    let Some(text) = text else { return State::Off };
    let mut in_entry = false;
    for line in text.lines() {
        let line = line.trim();
        if line.starts_with('[') {
            in_entry = line == "[Desktop Entry]";
            continue;
        }
        if in_entry && (line == "Hidden=true" || line == "X-GNOME-Autostart-enabled=false") {
            return State::TurnedOffElsewhere;
        }
    }
    State::On
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Single instance over the session bus** — `lin/instance.rs`:

```rust
//! One LinVPN per user and session: the first launch owns the bus name (`RequestName` with `DO_NOT_QUEUE`) and
//! serves `local.linvpn.Instance1.Open(aay files)` at `/local/linvpn/Instance`; a later launch calls `Open` with its
//! absolute file arguments (none: "show yourself") and exits. Files that arrive before the app listens wait in a
//! queue. `--after-update` (the updater's relaunch) waits up to 20 s for the old process to release the name.

use std::os::unix::ffi::{OsStrExt, OsStringExt};
use std::path::PathBuf;
use std::sync::{Arc, Mutex, PoisonError};
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};
use zbus::blocking::Connection;
use zbus::fdo::{RequestNameFlags, RequestNameReply};

type Listener = Box<dyn Fn(Vec<PathBuf>) + Send + Sync>;

#[derive(Default)]
struct Inbox {
    listener: Option<Listener>,
    queued: Vec<Vec<PathBuf>>,
}

struct Instance(Arc<Mutex<Inbox>>);

#[zbus::interface(name = "local.linvpn.Instance1")]
impl Instance {
    fn open(&self, files: Vec<Vec<u8>>) {
        let paths: Vec<PathBuf> = files
            .into_iter()
            .map(|b| PathBuf::from(std::ffi::OsString::from_vec(b)))
            .filter(|p| p.is_absolute())
            .collect();
        let mut inbox = self.0.lock().unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner);
        match &inbox.listener {
            Some(listen) => listen(paths),
            None => inbox.queued.push(paths),
        }
    }
}

pub enum Claim {
    First(InstanceGuard),
    Forwarded,
}

/// Keeps the name (and the connection serving `Open`) for the process' life.
pub struct InstanceGuard {
    _bus: Connection,
    inbox: Arc<Mutex<Inbox>>,
}

impl InstanceGuard {
    /// From now on `on_open` gets every forwarded launch (the queued ones first).
    pub fn listen(&self, on_open: Listener) {
        let mut inbox = self.inbox.lock().unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner);
        for paths in inbox.queued.drain(..) {
            on_open(paths);
        }
        inbox.listener = Some(on_open);
    }
}

const PATH: &str = "/local/linvpn/Instance";

pub fn claim(name: &str, files: &[PathBuf], wait_for_release: bool) -> zbus::Result<Claim> {
    let bus = crate::lin::bus::session()?;
    let inbox = Arc::new(Mutex::new(Inbox::default()));
    bus.object_server().at(PATH, Instance(inbox.clone()))?;
    let deadline = Instant::now() + if wait_for_release { Duration::from_secs(20) } else { Duration::ZERO };
    loop {
        let reply = bus.request_name_with_flags(name, RequestNameFlags::DoNotQueue.into())?;
        if matches!(reply, RequestNameReply::PrimaryOwner | RequestNameReply::AlreadyOwner) {
            return Ok(Claim::First(InstanceGuard { _bus: bus, inbox }));
        }
        if Instant::now() >= deadline {
            break;
        }
        std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(500));
    }
    let owner = zbus::blocking::Proxy::new(&bus, name, PATH, "local.linvpn.Instance1")?;
    let bytes: Vec<Vec<u8>> = files.iter().map(|p| p.as_os_str().as_bytes().to_vec()).collect();
    owner.call::<_, _, ()>("Open", &(bytes,))?;
    Ok(Claim::Forwarded)
}
```

(Check the pinned zbus' names: `request_name_with_flags` and `RequestNameFlags`; adapt.) A test, `crates/platform-linux/tests/lifecycle.rs` (private bus):

```rust
#![cfg(target_os = "linux")]

use macvpn_platform_linux::{Claim, claim_instance_for_tests};
use std::path::PathBuf;
use std::sync::mpsc;

#[test]
fn a_second_claim_forwards_its_files() {
    let name = format!("local.linvpn.Sandbox.htest{}", std::process::id());
    let Claim::First(guard) = claim_instance_for_tests(&name, &[], false).unwrap() else { panic!("not first") };
    // A second process is simulated by a second connection (the helper opens its own).
    let files = vec![PathBuf::from("/tmp/a.ovpn")];
    assert!(matches!(claim_instance_for_tests(&name, &files, false).unwrap(), Claim::Forwarded));
    let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel();
    guard.listen(Box::new(move |paths| tx.send(paths).unwrap()));
    assert_eq!(rx.recv_timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(2)).unwrap(), files, "queued before listen");
}
```

`claim_instance_for_tests` (in `lib.rs`, `#[doc(hidden)]`) opens a fresh `Connection::session()` for each call instead of the shared one, so the second claim is a second bus client. Export `Claim`, `InstanceGuard` and `claim_instance(sandbox: Option<&Path>, files, after_update) -> zbus::Result<Claim>` (which uses `pure::instance::bus_name`).

- [ ] **Step 4: The lifecycle** — `lin/lifecycle.rs`:

```rust
//! The app's quit request and the system's sleep and wake on Linux. SIGTERM (logout: systemd stops the session's
//! scope) and SIGINT go to the terminate handler on the GTK main loop (= the main thread): `Now` → the exit hook;
//! `Later` → the app answers through `reply_to_terminate`; `Cancel` → logged, LinVPN stays (logout follows with
//! SIGKILL after systemd's stop timeout). Sleep and wake: logind's `PrepareForSleep(true/false)` on the system bus.
//! Appearance: the portal's `color-scheme` changes.

use macvpn_platform_api::{AppLifecycle, TerminateReply};
use std::sync::{Mutex, PoisonError};
use zbus::blocking::Proxy;

type Hook = Box<dyn Fn() + Send + Sync>;

/// How the app exits (the app sets `AppHandle::exit(0)`; before it does: `std::process::exit(0)`).
static EXIT: Mutex<Option<Hook>> = Mutex::new(None);

pub fn set_exit_hook(hook: Hook) {
    *EXIT.lock().unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner) = Some(hook);
}

fn exit_now() {
    match EXIT.lock().unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner).as_ref() {
        Some(hook) => hook(),
        None => std::process::exit(0),
    }
}

pub struct Lifecycle;

impl AppLifecycle for Lifecycle {
    fn install_terminate_handler(&self, handler: Box<dyn Fn() -> TerminateReply + Send + Sync>) -> bool {
        let handler = std::sync::Arc::new(handler);
        let mut installed = true;
        for signal in [libc::SIGTERM, libc::SIGINT] {
            let handler = handler.clone();
            let source = glib::unix_signal_add_local(signal, move || {
                match handler() {
                    TerminateReply::Now => exit_now(),
                    TerminateReply::Later => {}
                    TerminateReply::Cancel => eprintln!("LinVPN: the quit request was declined"),
                }
                glib::ControlFlow::Continue
            });
            installed &= source.as_raw() != 0;
        }
        installed
    }

    fn reply_to_terminate(&self, terminate: bool) {
        if terminate {
            exit_now();
        }
    }

    fn observe_wake(&self, on_wake: Box<dyn Fn() + Send + Sync>) {
        watch_sleep(Box::new(move |sleeping| if !sleeping { on_wake() }));
    }

    fn observe_sleep(&self, on_sleep: Box<dyn Fn() + Send + Sync>) {
        watch_sleep(Box::new(move |sleeping| if sleeping { on_sleep() }));
    }

    fn observe_appearance(&self, on_change: Box<dyn Fn() + Send + Sync>) {
        crate::lin::portal::watch_color_scheme(on_change);
    }
}

fn watch_sleep(on: Box<dyn Fn(bool) + Send + Sync>) {
    let Ok(bus) = crate::lin::bus::system() else { return };
    let Ok(manager) = Proxy::new(&bus, "org.freedesktop.login1", "/org/freedesktop/login1", "org.freedesktop.login1.Manager")
    else {
        return;
    };
    let Ok(signals) = manager.receive_signal("PrepareForSleep") else { return };
    std::thread::Builder::new()
        .name("linvpn-sleep".into())
        .spawn(move || {
            for m in signals {
                if let Ok(sleeping) = m.body().deserialize::<bool>() {
                    on(sleeping);
                }
            }
        })
        .ok();
}
```

(`install_terminate_handler` must run on the main thread with the GTK main context: the app calls it in `setup`, which Tauri runs on the main thread. `glib::unix_signal_add_local`'s exact return type in glib 0.18 is a `SourceId`; replace the `as_raw` check with whatever tells success, or return `true`.) Export `set_exit_hook` from `lib.rs` (Linux). For the smoke run (Task 23) add, under `#[cfg(all(target_os = "linux", debug_assertions))] pub mod smoke`, `pub fn send_terminate()` that raises SIGTERM in this process (`// SAFETY: raise(2) with a constant signal number.` `unsafe { libc::raise(libc::SIGTERM) };`).

- [ ] **Step 5: Launch at login** — `lin/login_item.rs`:

```rust
//! "Launch at Login" through `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/autostart/linvpn.desktop` (`pure::autostart`). Only the installed
//! LinVPN (`/usr/bin/linvpn`) can toggle it; a development build shows why. GNOME has no settings page for it.

use crate::pure::autostart::{self, State};
use macvpn_core::l10n;
use macvpn_platform_api::{LoginItemView, LoginItems, PlatformError};
use std::path::PathBuf;

pub struct AutostartItem {
    pub file: PathBuf,
    pub installed: bool,
}

impl LoginItems for AutostartItem {
    fn state(&self) -> LoginItemView {
        let text = std::fs::read_to_string(&self.file).ok();
        let state = autostart::state_of(text.as_deref());
        if !self.installed {
            return LoginItemView {
                is_on: false,
                can_toggle: false,
                note: Some(l10n::tr("Launch at login is available when LinVPN is installed from its package.", &[])),
                shows_open_settings: false,
            };
        }
        LoginItemView {
            is_on: state == State::On,
            can_toggle: true,
            note: (state == State::TurnedOffElsewhere)
                .then(|| l10n::tr("Turned off by another app (Startup Applications).", &[])),
            shows_open_settings: false,
        }
    }

    fn set_enabled(&mut self, on: bool) -> Result<(), PlatformError> {
        if !self.installed {
            return Err(PlatformError::Failed(l10n::tr("Launch at login is available when LinVPN is installed from its package.", &[])));
        }
        if on {
            let dir = self.file.parent().ok_or_else(|| PlatformError::Failed("no autostart folder".into()))?;
            std::fs::create_dir_all(dir).map_err(|e| PlatformError::Failed(e.to_string()))?;
            macvpn_core::fsx::atomic_write(&self.file, autostart::desktop_file().as_bytes(), macvpn_core::fsx::Access::Shared)
                .map_err(|e| PlatformError::Failed(e.to_string()))
        } else {
            match std::fs::remove_file(&self.file) {
                Ok(()) => Ok(()),
                Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Ok(()),
                Err(e) => Err(PlatformError::Failed(e.to_string())),
            }
        }
    }

    fn unregister_if_registered(&mut self) -> Result<bool, PlatformError> {
        match std::fs::remove_file(&self.file) {
            Ok(()) => Ok(true),
            Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Ok(false),
            Err(e) => Err(PlatformError::Failed(e.to_string())),
        }
    }

    fn open_settings(&self) {}

    fn remove_stale_entries(&self) -> Result<(), PlatformError> {
        Ok(())
    }
}
```

(Check `fsx::atomic_write`'s real signature and use it; if it takes other arguments, adapt.) The accessor: `fn login_items(&self, home: &Path, _app: Option<&Path>, sandbox: bool) -> Box<dyn LoginItems>` → the sandbox: the no-op item; else `AutostartItem { file: self.xdg(home).autostart_file(), installed: self.system().app_location().is_some() }`.

- "Turned off by another app (Startup Applications)." → "Egy másik alkalmazás kikapcsolta (Indítási alkalmazások)."

```rust
#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
#[test]
fn toggling_writes_and_removes_the_file() {
    use macvpn_platform_api::LoginItems;
    use macvpn_platform_linux::AutostartItem;
    let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("linvpn-autostart-{}", uuid::Uuid::new_v4()));
    let mut item = AutostartItem { file: dir.join("autostart/linvpn.desktop"), installed: true };
    assert!(!item.state().is_on);
    item.set_enabled(true).unwrap();
    assert!(item.state().is_on);
    assert_eq!(std::fs::read_to_string(&item.file).unwrap(), macvpn_platform_linux::pure::autostart::desktop_file());
    item.set_enabled(false).unwrap();
    assert!(!item.file.exists());
    let dev = AutostartItem { file: dir.join("x.desktop"), installed: false };
    assert!(!dev.state().can_toggle);
}
```

## Task 17: the tray icon and the card panel

**Decided at CP-L0 (2026-10-29):** `TRAY = S`, `FORCE_X11 = false`. Skip Step 4 and every variant T part.

**CP-L0 amendments (they override the steps below where they differ):**
- **Clicks:** `activate` (a double click on GNOME) **and** `secondary_activate` (a middle click) both call `on_activate`, which **toggles** the panel (hide it when it is visible, else show it); the menu's "Open LinVPN" only shows it. A single left or right click shows the menu (the host's behaviour; nothing to code). The spike saw `ProvideXdgActivationToken(token)` before each `Activate`/`SecondaryActivate`/menu event: keep the last token and pass it to GTK before presenting the panel (`gtk_window_set_startup_id`); focus worked without it in the spike, but Mutter's focus-stealing rules may be stricter elsewhere. Check whether ksni 0.3.7 exposes the token; if not, note it and go on.
- **The panel on Wayland is the overlay** (Resolved points, "Panel placement"): `panel_position` stays as the pure rule and is used for the card's offset inside the overlay and for the Xwayland fallback; the window itself is not positioned. The overlay's window code (transparent, maximized, undecorated, input region = the card rectangle re-applied in `connect_size_allocate`, hide on focus loss) is Task 18's, in `platform/linux.rs` (it needs the `GtkWindow`); the input-region helper may live in `platform-linux::lin` if Task 18 prefers it there. The page's card rectangle comes from the UI (the popover's root element), in CSS pixels × the scale factor.
- **Dock icon:** while open, the overlay is a real toplevel and shows in the dock and Alt+Tab (both close it through focus loss): the window must carry LinVPN's app id / `.desktop` name so the dock shows LinVPN's icon, not a generic one.

**Goal:** the tray icon shows LinVPN's state (white, tinted by `tray_tint`), and the card panel opens at the top-right corner of the current monitor's work area: from a left click (variant S, when the host sends `Activate`) or from the menu's first item "Open LinVPN" (both variants); the menu's second item quits. The panel hides on focus loss. On Wayland the panel is the transparent overlay (CP-L0); XWayland is only the fallback.

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/platform-linux/src/pure/placement.rs`, `crates/platform-linux/tests/placement.rs`
- Create (variant S only): `crates/platform-linux/src/lin/tray.rs`
- Modify: `crates/platform-linux/src/{lib.rs,pure/mod.rs,lin/mod.rs}`, `crates/core/l10n/strings.json`

```rust
use macvpn_platform_linux::pure::placement::panel_position;

#[test]
fn top_right_of_the_work_area_with_an_8_px_margin() {
    // A 1920×1080 monitor whose work area starts below a 32 px top bar.
    assert_eq!(panel_position((0, 32, 1920, 1048), (330, 360)), (1582, 40));
    // A second monitor to the right.
```

`pure/placement.rs`:

```rust
//! The card panel's place: the top-right corner of the work area (physical pixels), 8 px in from the right edge and
//! below the top (GNOME's top bar is outside the work area), never left of the work area.

pub fn panel_position(work_area: (i32, i32, u32, u32), panel: (u32, u32)) -> (i32, i32) {
    let (x, y, width, _) = work_area;
    let left = x + width as i32 - panel.0 as i32 - 8;
    (left.max(x), y + 8)
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3 (variant S): the StatusNotifierItem** — `lin/tray.rs`:

```rust
//! The tray icon as an own StatusNotifierItem (ksni; spike L0-2, variant S): `ItemIsMenu = false`, so a host that
//! supports it sends `Activate` on a left click (→ `on_activate`: the panel); the menu (right click, or a left click
//! on a host that only shows menus) has "Open LinVPN" first and "Quit LinVPN". The icon is ARGB32 (big-endian), as
//! the SNI spec wants; labels are translated each time the host asks for the menu.

use macvpn_core::l10n;
use std::sync::Arc;

type Callback = Arc<dyn Fn() + Send + Sync>;

struct Item {
    icon: ksni::Icon,
    tooltip: String,
    on_activate: Callback,
    on_quit: Callback,
}

impl ksni::Tray for Item {
    const MENU_ON_ACTIVATE: bool = false;

    fn id(&self) -> String {
        "linvpn".into()
    }
    fn title(&self) -> String {
        "LinVPN".into()
    }
    fn icon_pixmap(&self) -> Vec<ksni::Icon> {
        vec![self.icon.clone()]
    }
    fn tool_tip(&self) -> ksni::ToolTip {
        ksni::ToolTip { title: self.tooltip.clone(), ..Default::default() }
    }
    fn activate(&mut self, _x: i32, _y: i32) {
        (self.on_activate)();
    }
    fn menu(&self) -> Vec<ksni::MenuItem<Self>> {
        vec![
            ksni::menu::StandardItem {
                label: l10n::tr("Open LinVPN", &[]),
                activate: Box::new(|t: &mut Item| (t.on_activate)()),
                ..Default::default()
            }
            .into(),
            ksni::MenuItem::Separator,
            ksni::menu::StandardItem {
                label: l10n::tr("Quit LinVPN", &[]),
                activate: Box::new(|t: &mut Item| (t.on_quit)()),
                ..Default::default()
            }
            .into(),
        ]
    }
}

/// RGBA (row-major, 8 bits per channel) → the SNI's ARGB32 big-endian.
pub fn argb(rgba: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8> {
    rgba.chunks_exact(4).flat_map(|p| [p[3], p[0], p[1], p[2]]).collect()
}

pub struct SniTray(ksni::blocking::Handle<Item>);

impl SniTray {
    pub fn spawn(
        rgba: &[u8],
        width: u32,
        height: u32,
        tooltip: &str,
        on_activate: Callback,
        on_quit: Callback,
    ) -> Result<SniTray, String> {
        let icon = ksni::Icon { width: width as i32, height: height as i32, data: argb(rgba) };
        let item = Item { icon, tooltip: tooltip.into(), on_activate, on_quit };
        ksni::blocking::TrayMethods::spawn(item).map(SniTray).map_err(|e| e.to_string())
    }

    pub fn set_icon(&self, rgba: &[u8], width: u32, height: u32, tooltip: &str) {
        let data = argb(rgba);
        let tooltip = tooltip.to_string();
        self.0.update(move |item| {
            item.icon = ksni::Icon { width: width as i32, height: height as i32, data };
            item.tooltip = tooltip;
        });
    }
}
```

(Adapt the `// ksni API` calls to the pinned version exactly as spike L0-2's code did.) Export `#[cfg(target_os = "linux")] pub use lin::tray::SniTray;` and a pure test of `argb` (move `argb` to `pure::placement`'s sibling `pure::pixels` if you want it tested on macOS too: `assert_eq!(argb(&[1, 2, 3, 4]), [4, 1, 2, 3])`).

**Variant T** needs no platform code: Task 18 builds Tauri's tray with the same two menu items.

- [ ] **Step 4 (`FORCE_X11 = true` only): the backend choice** — in `crates/platform-linux/src/lib.rs`:

```rust
/// Spike L0-2: GNOME on Wayland ignores where a client places its windows, so LinVPN asks GDK for its X11 backend
/// (XWayland), where the panel's position holds. A user's own `GDK_BACKEND` is kept. Must run first in `main`,
/// before any thread exists (`set_var` is not thread-safe).
#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
pub fn prefer_x11_backend() {
    if std::env::var_os("GDK_BACKEND").is_none() && std::env::var_os("WAYLAND_DISPLAY").is_some() {
        // SAFETY: called first in `main`, before any other thread exists (the function's contract).
        unsafe { std::env::set_var("GDK_BACKEND", "x11") };
    }
}
```

## Task 18: the app on Linux

**Goal:** `app/src-tauri` builds and runs on Linux with `LinPlatform`: the platform module `platform/linux.rs`, the Tauri config for LinVPN, the tray of the chosen variant, the panel at the corner, the theme from the portal, one instance per user, the files of a launch, SIGTERM to the quit flow. macOS and Windows unchanged (`tauri_config.rs`, every app test).

**Files:**
- Create: `app/src-tauri/src/platform/linux.rs`, `app/src-tauri/tauri.linux.conf.json`
- Modify: `app/src-tauri/Cargo.toml`, `app/src-tauri/src/platform/{mod.rs,macos.rs,windows.rs}` (three new facades with the old behaviour on macOS/Windows), `app/src-tauri/src/tray.rs`, `app/src-tauri/src/appearance.rs`, `app/src-tauri/src/main.rs` (the appearance observer), `app/src-tauri/tests/tauri_config.rs`

**CP-L0 amendments (2026-10-29; they override the steps below where they differ):**
- `TRAY_VARIANT_S = true`, `FORCE_X11 = false`: write only the variant S code; `harden_dll_search` does not call `prefer_x11_backend` (Task 17 Step 4 is skipped).
- **The overlay panel on Wayland** (Resolved points, "Panel placement"; spike L0-2 section (c)): on Linux the popover window is undecorated, `transparent: true` (in `tauri.linux.conf.json`), shown **maximized**, and the page draws the card at the top-right of a transparent body (Linux only: a body class set from `get_platform().os`); `platform/linux.rs` sets the GDK window's input region to the card rectangle (`gdk::Window::input_shape_combine_region` with a `cairo::Region` of the rectangle) after show **and** from `gtk::Window::connect_size_allocate` (GTK resets the shape on every allocation; without it the transparent window swallows every click). The UI reports the card's rectangle (CSS px × scale factor) whenever its size changes. `show_menu` does not call `set_position` on Wayland; `corner_position` is kept for the Xwayland fallback. Focus loss hides the panel (unchanged `hide_on_blur`); a click outside passes through. The toggle from the tray (`Activate`/`SecondaryActivate`) hides a visible panel. The manual checklist (Task 24) checks: no visible flash on open, the click-through, the dock icon is LinVPN's.
- **No GPU (WebKit's renderer):** the VM has no 3D acceleration (`bochs-drm`, `/dev/dri/card1` only, no `/dev/dri/renderD*`), and WebKitGTK 2.52 logs libEGL/MESA warnings there; the maintainer saw them and they are harmless. Decide here whether LinVPN sets `WEBKIT_DISABLE_DMABUF_RENDERER=1` (WebKitGTK's documented switch to its non-DMA-BUF path; the common fix for blank or flickering webviews on VMs and some NVIDIA drivers) when no render node exists: first check whether Tauri 2.12 / wry already sets it, then measure on the VM (warnings, a blank panel, the overlay's transparency, start time) with and without it. If it is set: in `platform-linux` next to the other `set_var` (first in `main`, before any thread; `unsafe` lives in the platform crate), only when the variable is unset by the user and `/dev/dri/renderD*` is absent, with a pure test of the decision. Record the result in the commit body. (`WEBKIT_DISABLE_COMPOSITING_MODE=1` is the heavier alternative; do not use it unless the measurement shows the overlay needs it.)

```rust
/// LinVPN's identity (tauri.linux.conf.json merged over the base, as the Tauri CLI does on Linux).
#[test]
fn linux_config_is_linvpn() {
    let base: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(include_str!("../tauri.conf.json")).unwrap();
    let linux: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(include_str!("../tauri.linux.conf.json")).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(linux["productName"], "LinVPN");
    assert_eq!(linux["mainBinaryName"], "linvpn");
    assert_eq!(linux["identifier"], "local.linvpn");
    assert_eq!(linux["bundle"]["active"], false, "the .deb is built by scripts/build-deb.sh");
    let menu = &linux["app"]["windows"][0];
    assert_eq!(menu["label"], "menu");
    assert_eq!(menu["title"], "LinVPN");
    // The popover keeps every other setting of the base (arrays are replaced whole by the merge).
    let base_menu = &base["app"]["windows"][0];
    for (key, value) in base_menu.as_object().unwrap() {
        if key != "title" {
            assert_eq!(&menu[key], value, "menu.{key}");
        }
    }
    assert!(linux.get("macOSPrivateApi").is_none() && linux["app"].get("macOSPrivateApi").is_none());
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: `platform/linux.rs`**

```rust
//! Linux: `LinPlatform` (`macvpn-platform-linux`) and the Tauri glue: the `GtkWindow` handle, one instance per user
//! and session bus (a second launch forwards its files), the files of this launch (`linvpn %F`), the exit hook for
//! SIGTERM, the tray of the variant spike L0-2 chose, and the popover's corner position.

use gtk::glib::object::ObjectType;
use macvpn_app_core::open_files::{ShowYourself, show_yourself as show_yourself_decision};
use macvpn_platform_api::{NativeWindow, Platform};
use macvpn_platform_linux::{Claim, InstanceGuard, LinPlatform};
use std::path::PathBuf;
use std::sync::{Arc, OnceLock};
use tauri::{AppHandle, Manager, Url};

use crate::state::AppState;

/// Spike L0-2's answers (CP-L0): the tray variant and the X11 backend.
const TRAY_VARIANT_S: bool = /* TRAY == S */ true;
const FORCE_X11: bool = /* FORCE_X11 */ false;

static PLATFORM: OnceLock<LinPlatform> = OnceLock::new();
static INSTANCE: OnceLock<InstanceGuard> = OnceLock::new();

pub fn init(sandbox: bool) -> &'static dyn Platform {
    PLATFORM.get_or_init(|| LinPlatform::new(sandbox))
}

pub fn get() -> Option<&'static dyn Platform> {
    PLATFORM.get().map(|p| p as &'static dyn Platform)
}

pub fn native_window(w: &tauri::WebviewWindow) -> Option<NativeWindow> {
    w.gtk_window().ok().map(|g| NativeWindow(g.as_ptr().cast()))
}

pub fn native_window_of(w: &tauri::Window) -> Option<NativeWindow> {
    w.gtk_window().ok().map(|g| NativeWindow(g.as_ptr().cast()))
}

fn launch_files() -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    macvpn_platform_linux::pure::instance::launch_files(std::env::args_os().skip(1))
}

fn urls(paths: &[PathBuf]) -> Vec<Url> {
    paths.iter().filter_map(|p| Url::from_file_path(p).ok()).collect()
}

/// Before Tauri: the first launch owns the session-bus name; a later one hands its files over and exits 0.
/// `--after-update` (the updater's relaunch) waits for the old process to let go. No session bus: run anyway (the
/// control socket's lock still keeps one control server).
pub fn claim_instance() -> bool {
    let sandbox = super::Environment::sandbox_home().ok().flatten();
    let after_update = std::env::args_os().any(|a| a == "--after-update");
    match macvpn_platform_linux::claim_instance(sandbox.as_deref(), &launch_files(), after_update) {
        Ok(Claim::First(guard)) => {
            let _ = INSTANCE.set(guard);
            true
        }
        Ok(Claim::Forwarded) => false,
        Err(e) => {
            eprintln!("LinVPN: single instance: {e}");
            true
        }
    }
}

pub fn configure_app(app: &mut tauri::App) {
    let handle = app.handle().clone();
    let exit = handle.clone();
    macvpn_platform_linux::set_exit_hook(Box::new(move || exit.exit(0)));
    let files = urls(&launch_files());
    if !files.is_empty() {
        let h = handle.clone();
        let _ = handle.run_on_main_thread(move || crate::open_files::opened(&h, &files));
    }
    if let Some(guard) = INSTANCE.get() {
        let h = handle.clone();
        guard.listen(Box::new(move |paths| {
            let target = h.clone();
            let _ = h.run_on_main_thread(move || {
                if paths.is_empty() {
                    show_yourself(&target);
                } else {
                    crate::open_files::opened(&target, &urls(&paths));
                }
            });
        }));
    }
}

/// As on Windows: an open window comes forward, else Connections (or the vault's window while it is closed).
fn show_yourself(app: &AppHandle) {
    let Some(state) = app.try_state::<Arc<AppState>>() else { return };
    let open = crate::dock::DOCK_WINDOWS.iter().any(|label| app.get_webview_window(label).is_some());
    match show_yourself_decision(open, crate::open_files::vault_ready(&state)) {
        ShowYourself::BringForward => crate::dock::reopen(app),
        ShowYourself::Connections => {
            let app = app.clone();
            std::thread::spawn(move || {
                if let Err(e) = crate::commands::open(&app, crate::manage::MANAGE_WINDOW) {
                    eprintln!("LinVPN: the Connections window: {e}");
                }
            });
        }
        ShowYourself::Vault => {
            let (app, state) = (app.clone(), state.inner().clone());
            std::thread::spawn(move || crate::open_files::show_vault_window(&app, &state));
        }
    }
}

/// GNOME shows every real window in its overview and dash by itself.
pub fn set_window_presence(_app: &AppHandle, _shown: bool) {}

pub fn on_run_event(_app: &AppHandle, _event: &tauri::RunEvent) {}

/// Linux: the GDK backend (spike L0-2), first thing in `main`, before any thread.
pub fn harden_dll_search() {
    if FORCE_X11 {
        macvpn_platform_linux::prefer_x11_backend();
    }
}

pub fn helper_mode() -> Option<i32> {
    None
}

pub fn relaunch() -> bool {
    false
}

#[cfg(debug_assertions)]
pub fn test_install_mode() -> Option<i32> {
    None
}

pub fn supported() -> Result<(), String> {
    Ok(())
}

pub fn uses_app_menu() -> bool {
    false
}

pub fn corner_position(work_area: (i32, i32, u32, u32), window: (u32, u32)) -> Option<(i32, i32)> {
    Some(macvpn_platform_linux::pure::placement::panel_position(work_area, window))
}
```

The tray functions, variant S (`TRAY_VARIANT_S = true`):

```rust
static OWN_TRAY: OnceLock<macvpn_platform_linux::SniTray> = OnceLock::new();

pub fn create_own_tray(app: &AppHandle, rgba: &[u8], width: u32, height: u32, tooltip: &str) -> bool {
    if !TRAY_VARIANT_S {
        return false;
    }
    let open = app.clone();
    let quit = app.clone();
    let tray = macvpn_platform_linux::SniTray::spawn(
        rgba,
        width,
        height,
        tooltip,
        Arc::new(move || {
            let app = open.clone();
            let _ = open.run_on_main_thread(move || crate::tray::toggle_menu(&app));
        }),
        Arc::new(move || {
            let app = quit.clone();
            let _ = quit.run_on_main_thread(move || {
                if let Some(state) = app.try_state::<Arc<AppState>>() {
                    crate::commands::request_quit(&app, &state, macvpn_app_core::quit::QuitOrigin::Menu);
                }
            });
        }),
    );
    match tray {
        Ok(tray) => OWN_TRAY.set(tray).is_ok(),
        Err(e) => {
            eprintln!("LinVPN: the tray icon: {e}");
            false
        }
    }
}

pub fn set_own_tray_icon(rgba: &[u8], width: u32, height: u32, tooltip: &str) -> bool {
    match OWN_TRAY.get() {
        Some(tray) => {
            tray.set_icon(rgba, width, height, tooltip);
            true
        }
        None => false,
    }
}

pub fn tray_menu(_app: &AppHandle) -> tauri::Result<Option<tauri::menu::Menu<tauri::Wry>>> {
    Ok(None)
}
```

```rust
pub fn tray_menu(app: &AppHandle) -> tauri::Result<Option<tauri::menu::Menu<tauri::Wry>>> {
    use macvpn_core::l10n::tr;
    use tauri::menu::{Menu, MenuItem, PredefinedMenuItem};
    let open = MenuItem::with_id(app, "linvpn-open", tr("Open LinVPN", &[]), true, None::<&str>)?;
    let quit = MenuItem::with_id(app, "linvpn-quit", tr("Quit LinVPN", &[]), true, None::<&str>)?;
    Ok(Some(Menu::with_items(app, &[&open, &PredefinedMenuItem::separator(app)?, &quit])?))
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: `tray.rs`: the own tray, the menu and the corner (no OS `cfg`)** — in `create`:

```rust
pub fn create(app: &AppHandle) -> tauri::Result<()> {
    let name = platform::get().product().name;
    if let Some(icon) = image(TrayIcon::Idle)
        && platform::create_own_tray(app, icon.rgba(), icon.width(), icon.height(), name)
    {
        return Ok(());
    }
    let mut builder = TrayIconBuilder::with_id(TRAY_ID)
        .icon_as_template(is_template())
        .tooltip(name)
        .show_menu_on_left_click(false)
        .on_tray_icon_event(|tray, event| handle_event(tray.app_handle(), event));
    if let Some(menu) = platform::tray_menu(app)? {
        builder = builder.menu(&menu).show_menu_on_left_click(true).on_menu_event(|app, event| match event.id().as_ref() {
            "linvpn-open" => show_menu(app),
            "linvpn-quit" => {
                if let Some(state) = app.try_state::<Arc<AppState>>() {
                    crate::commands::request_quit(app, &state, macvpn_app_core::quit::QuitOrigin::Menu);
                }
            }
            _ => {}
        });
    }
    if let Some(icon) = image(TrayIcon::Idle) {
        builder = builder.icon(icon);
    }
    builder.build(app)?;
    Ok(())
}
```

`set_icon`: after `apply_icon`, the tooltip goes to the own tray when there is one:

```rust
pub fn set_icon(app: &AppHandle, icon: TrayIcon, tooltip: Option<&str>) {
    let mut shown = SHOWN_ICON.lock().unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner);
    *shown = icon;
    let tip = tooltip.unwrap_or(platform::get().product().name);
    if let Some(img) = image(icon)
        && platform::set_own_tray_icon(img.rgba(), img.width(), img.height(), tip)
    {
        return;
    }
    apply_icon(app, icon);
    if let Some(tray) = app.tray_by_id(TRAY_ID) {
        let _ = tray.set_tooltip(Some(tip));
    }
}
```

```rust
/// The work area of the monitor under the pointer (or the primary one), physical pixels.
fn current_work_area(app: &AppHandle) -> Option<(i32, i32, u32, u32)> {
    let monitor = app
        .cursor_position()
        .ok()
        .and_then(|p| app.monitor_from_point(p.x, p.y).ok().flatten())
        .or_else(|| app.primary_monitor().ok().flatten())?;
    let area = monitor.work_area();
    Some((area.position.x, area.position.y, area.size.width, area.size.height))
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: The theme from the portal** — `appearance.rs`:

```rust
/// The native theme of a setting. `System` follows the system: `None` lets the toolkit do it (macOS, Windows); where
/// the toolkit does not follow it (Linux: `WindowChrome::system_dark`), the system's current choice.
pub fn theme(a: Appearance) -> Option<Theme> {
    theme_with(a, crate::platform::get().windows().system_dark())
}

pub fn theme_with(a: Appearance, system_dark: Option<bool>) -> Option<Theme> {
    match a {
        Appearance::System => system_dark.map(|dark| if dark { Theme::Dark } else { Theme::Light }),
        Appearance::Light => Some(Theme::Light),
        Appearance::Dark => Some(Theme::Dark),
    }
}

/// The system's light/dark choice changed: applied again while the setting is `System` and the platform reports the
/// choice itself (Linux). Nothing on macOS and Windows.
pub fn system_changed(app: &AppHandle) {
    if crate::platform::get().windows().system_dark().is_none() {
        return;
    }
    if let Some(state) = app.try_state::<Arc<AppState>>()
        && Appearance::load(state.prefs.as_ref()) == Appearance::System
    {
        apply(app, Appearance::System);
    }
}
```

```rust
    #[test]
    fn system_follows_the_os_and_the_others_force_a_theme() {
        assert_eq!(theme_with(Appearance::System, None), None);
        assert_eq!(theme_with(Appearance::System, Some(true)), Some(Theme::Dark));
        assert_eq!(theme_with(Appearance::System, Some(false)), Some(Theme::Light));
        for system in [None, Some(true), Some(false)] {
            assert_eq!(theme_with(Appearance::Light, system), Some(Theme::Light));
            assert_eq!(theme_with(Appearance::Dark, system), Some(Theme::Dark));
        }
    }
```

(Adjust the old Windows condition to this form only if it is equivalent: the old rule was "`only` empty and no `windows` variant"; an entry with `only: ["macos"]` passes both. Keep the assertion message: "macOS terms without `only` or a variant".) Add after `no_macos_term_reaches_windows`:

```rust
/// What LinVPN shows: every text resolved for Linux, in both languages, names no macOS facility.
#[test]
fn no_macos_term_reaches_linux() {
    use macvpn_core::TargetOs;
    use macvpn_core::l10n::{AppLanguage, TextPlatform};
    let linux = TextPlatform { os: TargetOs::Linux, product: "LinVPN" };
    let mut offenders = Vec::new();
    for entry in l10n::table() {
        if !entry.shown_on(TargetOs::Linux) {
            continue;
        }
        for language in [AppLanguage::En, AppLanguage::Hu] {
            let text = l10n::tr_for(linux, language, &entry.key, &[]);
            for term in MACOS_TERMS {
                if contains_term(&text, term) {
                    offenders.push(format!("{term}: {text}"));
                }
            }
        }
    }
    assert!(offenders.is_empty(), "macOS terms in Linux texts:\n{}", offenders.join("\n"));
}

/// Windows facilities never reach a Linux text either (a variant copied from Windows by mistake).
const WINDOWS_TERMS: &[&str] = &[
    "Credential Manager",
    "Hitelesítőadat-kezelő",
    "File Explorer",
    "Fájlkezelő",
    "notification area",
    "értesítési terület",
    "taskbar",
    "tálca",
    "this PC",
    "Settings → Apps",
    "Gépház",
    "UAC",
    "WinVPN Service",
];

#[test]
fn no_windows_term_reaches_linux() {
    use macvpn_core::TargetOs;
    use macvpn_core::l10n::{AppLanguage, TextPlatform};
    let linux = TextPlatform { os: TargetOs::Linux, product: "LinVPN" };
    let mut offenders = Vec::new();
    for entry in l10n::table() {
        if !entry.shown_on(TargetOs::Linux) {
            continue;
        }
        for language in [AppLanguage::En, AppLanguage::Hu] {
            let text = l10n::tr_for(linux, language, &entry.key, &[]);
            for term in WINDOWS_TERMS {
                if contains_term(&text, term) {
                    offenders.push(format!("{term}: {text}"));
                }
            }
        }
    }
    assert!(offenders.is_empty(), "Windows terms in Linux texts:\n{}", offenders.join("\n"));
}
```

## Task 20: the update check and the self-update through pkexec

**Goal:** an installed LinVPN finds a newer release (the curl layer over `/usr/bin/curl`), downloads its architecture's `.deb`, `.sha256` and `.sig` into its private updates folder, verifies hash and signature under `LinVpnV1`, asks the quit questions, and runs `pkexec /usr/libexec/linvpn/linvpn-install-update`, which re-verifies everything as root on its own copy and installs with apt; then LinVPN restarts as the new version. HTTPS team catalogs work through the same curl layer.

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/core/src/deb_update.rs`, `crates/core/tests/deb_update.rs`
- Create: `crates/helper/src/linux/mod.rs`, `crates/helper/src/linux/install_update.rs`, `crates/helper/src/linux/main_install_update.rs`, `crates/helper/tests/install_update.rs`
- Create: `crates/platform-linux/src/pure/{update.rs,update_check.rs}`, `crates/platform-linux/tests/update.rs`
- Create: `crates/platform-linux/src/lin/{command.rs,curl_runner.rs,updater.rs}`
- Create: `packaging/linux/local.linvpn.update.policy`
- Modify: `crates/core/src/lib.rs`, `crates/helper/{Cargo.toml,src/lib.rs}`, `crates/platform-linux/src/{lib.rs,pure/mod.rs,lin/mod.rs,lin/platform.rs}` (the `updater`, `release_source`, `https_catalog_fetcher` accessors and `SystemInfo::update_info`), `crates/core/l10n/strings.json`

```rust
use macvpn_core::deb_update::{self, Fields, StepArgs, StepError};
use std::path::PathBuf;

#[test]
fn asset_names() {
    assert_eq!(deb_update::deb_arch("x86_64"), Some("amd64"));
    assert_eq!(deb_update::deb_arch("aarch64"), Some("arm64"));
    assert_eq!(deb_update::deb_arch("riscv64"), None);
    assert_eq!(deb_update::asset("2.1.0", "amd64"), "linvpn_2.1.0_amd64.deb");
}

#[test]
fn dpkg_deb_fields() {
    let out = "Package: linvpn\nVersion: 2.1.0\nArchitecture: amd64\n";
    assert_eq!(
        deb_update::parse_fields(out),
        Some(Fields { package: "linvpn".into(), version: "2.1.0".into(), architecture: "amd64".into() })
    );
    assert_eq!(deb_update::parse_fields("Package: linvpn\nVersion: 2.1.0\n"), None);
    assert_eq!(deb_update::parse_fields("Package: linvpn\nPackage: other\nVersion: 2.1.0\nArchitecture: amd64\n"), None);
}

#[test]
fn version_order() {
    assert!(deb_update::is_newer("2.1.0", None));
    assert!(deb_update::is_newer("2.1.0", Some("2.0.5")));
    assert!(deb_update::is_newer("2.10.0", Some("2.9.9")));
    assert!(!deb_update::is_newer("2.0.5", Some("2.0.5")));
    assert!(!deb_update::is_newer("2.0.4", Some("2.0.5")));
    assert!(!deb_update::is_newer("2.1", Some("2.0.5")));
    assert!(!deb_update::is_newer("2.1.0", Some("garbage")));
}

#[test]
fn the_package_must_be_ours_this_architecture_the_signed_version_and_newer() {
    let ok = Fields { package: "linvpn".into(), version: "2.1.0".into(), architecture: "amd64".into() };
    assert_eq!(deb_update::check(&ok, "2.1.0", "amd64", Some("2.0.5")), Ok(()));
    let other = Fields { package: "evil".into(), ..ok.clone() };
    assert_eq!(deb_update::check(&other, "2.1.0", "amd64", None), Err(StepError::WrongPackage));
    assert_eq!(deb_update::check(&ok, "2.1.0", "arm64", None), Err(StepError::WrongArchitecture));
    assert_eq!(deb_update::check(&ok, "2.1.1", "amd64", None), Err(StepError::WrongVersion));
    assert_eq!(deb_update::check(&ok, "2.1.0", "amd64", Some("2.1.0")), Err(StepError::NotNewer));
}

#[test]
fn step_arguments_exactly() {
    let args: Vec<String> = ["--deb", "/home/a/.cache/LinVPN/updates/linvpn_2.1.0_amd64.deb", "--sig", "/home/a/.cache/LinVPN/updates/linvpn_2.1.0_amd64.deb.sig", "--version", "2.1.0", "--asset", "linvpn_2.1.0_amd64.deb"]
        .map(String::from)
        .to_vec();
    assert_eq!(
        deb_update::parse_step_args(&args),
        Some(StepArgs {
            deb: PathBuf::from("/home/a/.cache/LinVPN/updates/linvpn_2.1.0_amd64.deb"),
            sig: PathBuf::from("/home/a/.cache/LinVPN/updates/linvpn_2.1.0_amd64.deb.sig"),
            version: "2.1.0".into(),
            asset: "linvpn_2.1.0_amd64.deb".into(),
        })
    );
    assert_eq!(deb_update::parse_step_args(&args[..7]), None);
    let mut relative = args.clone();
    relative[1] = "linvpn.deb".into();
    assert_eq!(deb_update::parse_step_args(&relative), None);
    let mut extra = args.clone();
    extra.push("--force".into());
    assert_eq!(deb_update::parse_step_args(&extra), None);
}

#[test]
fn exit_codes_are_distinct_and_never_pkexecs() {
    let codes: Vec<i32> = StepError::ALL.iter().map(|e| e.code()).collect();
    let mut unique = codes.clone();
    unique.sort();
    unique.dedup();
    assert_eq!(unique.len(), codes.len());
    assert!(codes.iter().all(|c| (10..=40).contains(c)), "never 0, 126 or 127 (pkexec's own)");
}
```

- [ ] **Step 2: `crates/core/src/deb_update.rs`** (add `pub mod deb_update;` to `lib.rs`):

```rust
//! LinVPN's `.deb` update rules, shared by the app (before it asks for the password) and the root step
//! `linvpn-install-update` (which checks everything again on its own copy): the asset name, `dpkg-deb --field`'s
//! output, the version order (numeric X.Y.Z only), the root step's exact arguments and exit codes. Pure: no `cfg`, no
//! file system.

use std::path::PathBuf;

pub const PACKAGE: &str = "linvpn";

/// Rust's `std::env::consts::ARCH` → Debian's architecture name.
pub fn deb_arch(rust_arch: &str) -> Option<&'static str> {
    match rust_arch {
        "x86_64" => Some("amd64"),
        "aarch64" => Some("arm64"),
        _ => None,
    }
}

pub fn asset(version: &str, deb_arch: &str) -> String {
    format!("{PACKAGE}_{version}_{deb_arch}.deb")
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Fields {
    pub package: String,
    pub version: String,
    pub architecture: String,
}

/// `dpkg-deb --field <deb> Package Version Architecture`: each field exactly once.
pub fn parse_fields(output: &str) -> Option<Fields> {
    let (mut package, mut version, mut architecture) = (None, None, None);
    for line in output.lines() {
        let (key, value) = line.split_once(": ")?;
        let slot = match key {
            "Package" => &mut package,
            "Version" => &mut version,
            "Architecture" => &mut architecture,
            _ => return None,
        };
        if slot.replace(value.trim().to_string()).is_some() {
            return None;
        }
    }
    Some(Fields { package: package?, version: version?, architecture: architecture? })
}

fn numeric(version: &str) -> Option<[u64; 3]> {
    let parts: Vec<u64> = version.split('.').map(|p| p.parse().ok()).collect::<Option<_>>()?;
    parts.try_into().ok()
}

/// `candidate` is a plain X.Y.Z newer than `installed` (none installed: any valid candidate).
pub fn is_newer(candidate: &str, installed: Option<&str>) -> bool {
    let Some(c) = numeric(candidate) else { return false };
    match installed {
        None => true,
        Some(i) => numeric(i).is_some_and(|i| c > i),
    }
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum StepError {
    BadArguments,
    NotRoot,
    UnsafeFile,
    BadSignature,
    WrongPackage,
    WrongArchitecture,
    WrongVersion,
    NotNewer,
    ToolFailed,
    InstallFailed,
}

impl StepError {
    pub const ALL: [StepError; 10] = [
        StepError::BadArguments,
        StepError::NotRoot,
        StepError::UnsafeFile,
        StepError::BadSignature,
        StepError::WrongPackage,
        StepError::WrongArchitecture,
        StepError::WrongVersion,
        StepError::NotNewer,
        StepError::ToolFailed,
        StepError::InstallFailed,
    ];

    /// The root step's exit code (never 0, nor pkexec's own 126 and 127).
    pub fn code(self) -> i32 {
        match self {
            StepError::BadArguments => 10,
            StepError::NotRoot => 11,
            StepError::UnsafeFile => 12,
            StepError::BadSignature => 13,
            StepError::WrongPackage => 14,
            StepError::WrongArchitecture => 15,
            StepError::WrongVersion => 16,
            StepError::NotNewer => 17,
            StepError::ToolFailed => 18,
            StepError::InstallFailed => 19,
        }
    }

    pub fn from_code(code: i32) -> Option<StepError> {
        StepError::ALL.into_iter().find(|e| e.code() == code)
    }
}

/// The package must be ours, of this machine's architecture, exactly the signed version, and newer than the installed
/// one.
pub fn check(fields: &Fields, signed_version: &str, dpkg_arch: &str, installed: Option<&str>) -> Result<(), StepError> {
    if fields.package != PACKAGE {
        return Err(StepError::WrongPackage);
    }
    if fields.architecture != dpkg_arch {
        return Err(StepError::WrongArchitecture);
    }
    if fields.version != signed_version {
        return Err(StepError::WrongVersion);
    }
    if !is_newer(&fields.version, installed) {
        return Err(StepError::NotNewer);
    }
    Ok(())
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct StepArgs {
    pub deb: PathBuf,
    pub sig: PathBuf,
    pub version: String,
    pub asset: String,
}

/// Exactly `--deb <abs> --sig <abs> --version X.Y.Z --asset <name>`, in this order.
pub fn parse_step_args(args: &[String]) -> Option<StepArgs> {
    let [d, deb, s, sig, v, version, a, asset] = args else { return None };
    if d != "--deb" || s != "--sig" || v != "--version" || a != "--asset" {
        return None;
    }
    let (deb, sig) = (PathBuf::from(deb), PathBuf::from(sig));
    if !deb.is_absolute() || !sig.is_absolute() || numeric(version).is_none() {
        return None;
    }
    Some(StepArgs { deb, sig, version: version.clone(), asset: asset.clone() })
}

/// The arguments the app passes ([`parse_step_args`] reads them back).
pub fn step_args(deb: &std::path::Path, sig: &std::path::Path, version: &str, asset: &str) -> Vec<std::ffi::OsString> {
    vec!["--deb".into(), deb.into(), "--sig".into(), sig.into(), "--version".into(), version.into(), "--asset".into(), asset.into()]
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: The root step (failing tests first)** — `crates/helper/tests/install_update.rs` drives the step's logic with fake tools and a throwaway key (the real `main` needs root; the logic takes its environment as parameters):

```rust
#![cfg(target_os = "linux")]

use ed25519_dalek::{Signer, SigningKey};
use macvpn_core::deb_update::{StepArgs, StepError};
use macvpn_core::update::{SignatureDomain, UpdateSignature};
use macvpn_helper::linux::install_update::{Env, run};
use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};
use std::cell::RefCell;
use std::os::unix::fs::{MetadataExt, PermissionsExt};
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::rc::Rc;

const VERSION: &str = "2.1.0";
const ASSET: &str = "linvpn_2.1.0_amd64.deb";
const FIELDS: &str = "Package: linvpn\nVersion: 2.1.0\nArchitecture: amd64\n";

type AptCalls = Rc<RefCell<Vec<(PathBuf, Vec<u8>)>>>;

fn key() -> SigningKey {
    SigningKey::from_bytes(&[7; 32])
}

/// A private folder of this user with the package `content` and a signature over `signed` (default: `content`).
fn case(content: &[u8], signed: Option<&[u8]>) -> (PathBuf, StepArgs) {
    let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("linvpn-step-{}", uuid::Uuid::new_v4()));
    std::fs::create_dir(&dir).unwrap();
    std::fs::set_permissions(&dir, std::fs::Permissions::from_mode(0o700)).unwrap();
    let deb = dir.join(ASSET);
    std::fs::write(&deb, content).unwrap();
    let sha: String = Sha256::digest(signed.unwrap_or(content)).iter().map(|b| format!("{b:02x}")).collect();
    let message = UpdateSignature::message_for(SignatureDomain::LinVpnV1, ASSET, VERSION, &sha).unwrap();
    let sig = dir.join(format!("{ASSET}.sig"));
    std::fs::write(&sig, macvpn_core::swift_base64::encode(&key().sign(&message).to_bytes()) + "\n").unwrap();
    let args = StepArgs { deb, sig, version: VERSION.into(), asset: ASSET.into() };
    (dir, args)
}

fn env(dir: &Path, fields: impl Fn(&Path) -> Option<String> + 'static, installed: Option<&'static str>, apt: AptCalls) -> Env {
    Env {
        caller_uid: std::fs::metadata(dir).unwrap().uid(),
        staging: dir.join("staging"),
        keys: vec![key().verifying_key().to_bytes()],
        dpkg_arch: Box::new(|| Some("amd64".into())),
        fields: Box::new(fields),
        installed: Box::new(move || installed.map(String::from)),
        apt_install: Box::new(move |deb| {
            apt.borrow_mut().push((deb.to_path_buf(), std::fs::read(deb).unwrap()));
            true
        }),
    }
}

fn fixed(text: &'static str) -> impl Fn(&Path) -> Option<String> {
    move |_| Some(text.to_string())
}

#[test]
fn a_good_package_is_installed_from_roots_copy() {
    let (dir, args) = case(b"package A", None);
    let apt = AptCalls::default();
    assert_eq!(run(&args, &env(&dir, fixed(FIELDS), Some("2.0.5"), apt.clone())), Ok(()));
    let calls = apt.borrow();
    assert_eq!(calls.len(), 1);
    assert!(calls[0].0.starts_with(dir.join("staging")), "{}", calls[0].0.display());
    assert_ne!(calls[0].0, args.deb);
    assert_eq!(calls[0].1, b"package A");
    assert_eq!(std::fs::read_dir(dir.join("staging")).unwrap().count(), 0, "the copy is removed");
}

#[test]
fn a_bad_signature_installs_nothing() {
    let (dir, args) = case(b"package A", Some(b"another package"));
    let apt = AptCalls::default();
    assert_eq!(run(&args, &env(&dir, fixed(FIELDS), None, apt.clone())), Err(StepError::BadSignature));
    assert!(apt.borrow().is_empty());
}

#[test]
fn a_file_swapped_after_the_check_is_not_installed() {
    let (dir, args) = case(b"package A", None);
    let apt = AptCalls::default();
    let user_file = args.deb.clone();
    // The swap happens after the copy and the signature check (when the fields are read): too late.
    let swapping = move |_: &Path| {
        std::fs::write(&user_file, b"package B").unwrap();
        Some(FIELDS.to_string())
    };
    assert_eq!(run(&args, &env(&dir, swapping, None, apt.clone())), Ok(()));
    assert_eq!(apt.borrow()[0].1, b"package A");
}

#[test]
fn wrong_package_architecture_version_or_older_are_refused() {
    for (fields, installed, expected) in [
        ("Package: evil\nVersion: 2.1.0\nArchitecture: amd64\n", None, StepError::WrongPackage),
        ("Package: linvpn\nVersion: 2.1.0\nArchitecture: arm64\n", None, StepError::WrongArchitecture),
        ("Package: linvpn\nVersion: 2.1.1\nArchitecture: amd64\n", None, StepError::WrongVersion),
        (FIELDS, Some("2.1.0"), StepError::NotNewer),
    ] {
        let (dir, args) = case(b"package A", None);
        let apt = AptCalls::default();
        assert_eq!(run(&args, &env(&dir, fixed(fields), installed, apt.clone())), Err(expected), "{fields}");
        assert!(apt.borrow().is_empty());
    }
}

#[test]
fn the_other_architectures_asset_is_refused_before_any_file_is_opened() {
    let (dir, mut args) = case(b"package A", None);
    args.asset = "linvpn_2.1.0_arm64.deb".into();
    let apt = AptCalls::default();
    assert_eq!(run(&args, &env(&dir, fixed(FIELDS), None, apt.clone())), Err(StepError::WrongArchitecture));
}

#[test]
fn a_symlink_or_an_open_folder_is_refused() {
    let (dir, args) = case(b"package A", None);
    let real = dir.join("real.deb");
    std::fs::rename(&args.deb, &real).unwrap();
    std::os::unix::fs::symlink(&real, &args.deb).unwrap();
    let apt = AptCalls::default();
    assert_eq!(run(&args, &env(&dir, fixed(FIELDS), None, apt.clone())), Err(StepError::UnsafeFile));

    let (dir, args) = case(b"package A", None);
    std::fs::set_permissions(&dir, std::fs::Permissions::from_mode(0o770)).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(run(&args, &env(&dir, fixed(FIELDS), None, apt.clone())), Err(StepError::UnsafeFile));
    assert!(apt.borrow().is_empty());
}
```

`crates/helper/src/linux/install_update.rs`:

```rust
//! `linvpn-install-update`: LinVPN's only code that runs as root in L-a, started by pkexec under the polkit action
//! `local.linvpn.update` (`/usr/libexec/linvpn/linvpn-install-update --deb … --sig … --version … --asset …`).
//!
//! It trusts nothing from the caller: it opens the caller's two files without following links (regular files of the
//! calling user, `PKEXEC_UID`, in a folder of that user with no group or other access), copies the package into a
//! fresh root-owned 0700 folder under /var/cache/linvpn-update while hashing it, and on that copy verifies the
//! Ed25519 signature (`linvpn-update-v1`, the asset name, the version, the hash; the keys embedded in the core),
//! that the asset is this machine's architecture, and `dpkg-deb --field`'s Package / Version / Architecture
//! (`deb_update::check`: ours, this architecture, the signed version, newer than the installed one). Only then:
//! `apt-get install -y <copy>` with a clean environment. The copy is removed afterwards. Exit codes:
//! `deb_update::StepError::code`; 0 = installed.

use macvpn_core::deb_update::{self, StepArgs, StepError};
use macvpn_core::update::{SignatureDomain, UpdateSignature};
use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};
use std::fs::{File, OpenOptions};
use std::io::{Read, Write};
use std::os::unix::fs::{DirBuilderExt, MetadataExt, OpenOptionsExt};
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

const MAX_DEB: u64 = 512 * 1024 * 1024;
const STAGING: &str = "/var/cache/linvpn-update";

/// What the step needs from the system, as parameters (tests pass fakes).
pub struct Env {
    pub caller_uid: u32,
    pub staging: PathBuf,
    pub keys: Vec<[u8; 32]>,
    /// `dpkg --print-architecture`.
    pub dpkg_arch: Box<dyn Fn() -> Option<String>>,
    /// `dpkg-deb --field <deb> Package Version Architecture`.
    pub fields: Box<dyn Fn(&Path) -> Option<String>>,
    /// The installed version of `linvpn` (`dpkg-query`); `None`: not installed.
    pub installed: Box<dyn Fn() -> Option<String>>,
    /// `apt-get install -y <deb>`; true: installed.
    pub apt_install: Box<dyn Fn(&Path) -> bool>,
}

fn open_callers(path: &Path, uid: u32, max: u64) -> Result<File, StepError> {
    let parent = path.parent().ok_or(StepError::UnsafeFile)?;
    let dir = std::fs::symlink_metadata(parent).map_err(|_| StepError::UnsafeFile)?;
    if !dir.is_dir() || dir.uid() != uid || dir.mode() & 0o077 != 0 {
        return Err(StepError::UnsafeFile);
    }
    let file = OpenOptions::new()
        .read(true)
        .custom_flags(libc::O_NOFOLLOW | libc::O_CLOEXEC)
        .open(path)
        .map_err(|_| StepError::UnsafeFile)?;
    let meta = file.metadata().map_err(|_| StepError::UnsafeFile)?;
    if !meta.is_file() || meta.uid() != uid || meta.len() > max || meta.nlink() != 1 {
        return Err(StepError::UnsafeFile);
    }
    Ok(file)
}

/// A new 0700 folder under `staging` (created 0700 if missing), named with 16 random hex digits.
fn new_staging_dir(staging: &Path) -> Result<PathBuf, StepError> {
    std::fs::DirBuilder::new().mode(0o700).recursive(true).create(staging).map_err(|_| StepError::ToolFailed)?;
    let mut bytes = [0u8; 8];
    macvpn_unixsys::random::fill(&mut bytes).map_err(|_| StepError::ToolFailed)?;
    let name: String = bytes.iter().map(|b| format!("{b:02x}")).collect();
    let dir = staging.join(name);
    std::fs::DirBuilder::new().mode(0o700).create(&dir).map_err(|_| StepError::ToolFailed)?;
    Ok(dir)
}

pub fn run(args: &StepArgs, env: &Env) -> Result<(), StepError> {
    let dpkg_arch = (env.dpkg_arch)().ok_or(StepError::ToolFailed)?;
    if args.asset != deb_update::asset(&args.version, &dpkg_arch) {
        return Err(StepError::WrongArchitecture);
    }
    let mut source = open_callers(&args.deb, env.caller_uid, MAX_DEB)?;
    let mut sig_file = open_callers(&args.sig, env.caller_uid, UpdateSignature::MAX_SIGNATURE_FILE_SIZE as u64)?;
    let mut signature = String::new();
    sig_file.read_to_string(&mut signature).map_err(|_| StepError::BadSignature)?;

    let dir = new_staging_dir(&env.staging)?;
    let copy = dir.join(&args.asset);
    let result = (|| {
        let mut out = OpenOptions::new()
            .write(true)
            .create_new(true)
            .mode(0o600)
            .custom_flags(libc::O_NOFOLLOW | libc::O_CLOEXEC)
            .open(&copy)
            .map_err(|_| StepError::ToolFailed)?;
        let mut hasher = Sha256::new();
        let mut buf = vec![0u8; 1 << 16];
        loop {
            let n = source.read(&mut buf).map_err(|_| StepError::UnsafeFile)?;
            if n == 0 {
                break;
            }
            hasher.update(&buf[..n]);
            out.write_all(&buf[..n]).map_err(|_| StepError::ToolFailed)?;
        }
        out.sync_all().map_err(|_| StepError::ToolFailed)?;
        let sha: String = hasher.finalize().iter().map(|b| format!("{b:02x}")).collect();
        if !UpdateSignature::verify_for(SignatureDomain::LinVpnV1, &args.asset, &args.version, &sha, &signature, &env.keys) {
            return Err(StepError::BadSignature);
        }
        let fields = (env.fields)(&copy).and_then(|o| deb_update::parse_fields(&o)).ok_or(StepError::ToolFailed)?;
        deb_update::check(&fields, &args.version, &dpkg_arch, (env.installed)().as_deref())?;
        if !(env.apt_install)(&copy) {
            return Err(StepError::InstallFailed);
        }
        Ok(())
    })();
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
    result
}

/// The real environment: root, the tools at their absolute paths, a clean environment for each.
pub fn real_env() -> Result<Env, StepError> {
    // SAFETY: `geteuid` takes no argument and cannot fail.
    if unsafe { libc::geteuid() } != 0 {
        return Err(StepError::NotRoot);
    }
    let caller_uid: u32 = std::env::var("PKEXEC_UID").ok().and_then(|u| u.parse().ok()).ok_or(StepError::BadArguments)?;
    let tool = |program: &'static str, args: Vec<std::ffi::OsString>| -> Option<String> {
        let out = std::process::Command::new(program)
            .args(args)
            .env_clear()
            .env("PATH", "/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin")
            .env("LC_ALL", "C")
            .stdin(std::process::Stdio::null())
            .output()
            .ok()?;
        out.status.success().then(|| String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).into_owned())
    };
    Ok(Env {
        caller_uid,
        staging: PathBuf::from(STAGING),
        keys: UpdateSignature::trusted_keys(),
        dpkg_arch: Box::new(move || tool("/usr/bin/dpkg", vec!["--print-architecture".into()]).map(|s| s.trim().to_string())),
        fields: Box::new(move |deb| {
            tool("/usr/bin/dpkg-deb", vec!["--field".into(), deb.into(), "Package".into(), "Version".into(), "Architecture".into()])
        }),
        installed: Box::new(move || {
            tool("/usr/bin/dpkg-query", vec!["-W".into(), "-f=${Version}".into(), "linvpn".into()]).filter(|v| !v.is_empty())
        }),
        apt_install: Box::new(|deb| {
            std::process::Command::new("/usr/bin/apt-get")
                .args(["install", "-y"])
                .arg(deb)
                .env_clear()
                .env("PATH", "/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin")
                .env("LC_ALL", "C")
                .env("DEBIAN_FRONTEND", "noninteractive")
                .stdin(std::process::Stdio::null())
                .status()
                .is_ok_and(|s| s.success())
        }),
    })
}

/// The binary's `main`: its exit code.
pub fn main() -> i32 {
    let args: Vec<String> = match std::env::args_os().skip(1).map(|a| a.into_string()).collect() {
        Ok(a) => a,
        Err(_) => return StepError::BadArguments.code(),
    };
    let Some(args) = deb_update::parse_step_args(&args) else { return StepError::BadArguments.code() };
    let env = match real_env() {
        Ok(env) => env,
        Err(e) => return e.code(),
    };
    match run(&args, &env) {
        Ok(()) => 0,
        Err(e) => {
            eprintln!("linvpn-install-update: refused ({e:?})");
            e.code()
        }
    }
}
```

`crates/helper/src/linux/mod.rs` (the OS `cfg` lives inside the backend's folder, as the cfg rule wants):

```rust
//! The Linux backend of the helper: no `mvpn` (LinVPN drives NetworkManager), only LinVPN's root update step
//! ([`install_update`]). Linux only: elsewhere this module is empty.

#![cfg(target_os = "linux")]

pub mod install_update;
```

`crates/helper/src/lib.rs`: `pub mod linux;` (beside `macos` and `windows`; the doc names the Linux backend). The binary's entry is a second `[[bin]]` in `crates/helper/Cargo.toml`, inside the backend's folder (a `src/bin/` file would be outside the cfg rule's allowed paths): `[[bin]] name = "linvpn-install-update"`, `path = "src/linux/main_install_update.rs"`, with:

```rust
//! The root update step's binary (`/usr/libexec/linvpn/linvpn-install-update`). Linux only; elsewhere it exits 2.

#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
fn main() {
    std::process::exit(macvpn_helper::linux::install_update::main())
}

#[cfg(not(target_os = "linux"))]
fn main() {
    eprintln!("linvpn-install-update is LinVPN's (Linux)");
    std::process::exit(2)
}
```

Another code: `Err(outcome message)`, notice `failed <version> <code>`.
  
  `launch_housekeeping(app, home, noticed)`: read and parse `<updates>/notice`; return it unless `noticed` is the same run id (the run id is the notice line itself); remove the `.deb`, `.sig`, `.sha256` and logs older than 7 days; return the removed paths. `cache_directory(home)` → `Some(updates_dir)`. The sandbox's updater: `Err(not_yet())`-style "not in the sandbox" as WinVPN's sandbox does (the smoke run has its own update path).

Cases: exit 0 → `HelperLaunched`, notice `ok`, relaunch started with `--after-update`; 126 → `Cancelled`; 13 → `Err` with "code 13"; a bad `.sha256` → `Err`, pkexec never started; `confirm_quit` false → `Cancelled`, pkexec never started.

## Task 21: the `.deb` and its release gates

**Goal:** `scripts/build-deb.sh` builds `dist/linux/linvpn_<version>_<arch>.deb` for the machine's architecture with every gate of spec 7 and "Resolved points": the package name, version and architecture; Depends (from `dpkg-shlibdeps` plus NetworkManager, curl, pkexec and a StatusNotifierItem host) and Recommends (the NM plugins); the desktop entry, the MIME types, the polkit action, the icons, the copyright and notices; no maintainer scripts; no debug code; SQLCipher statically on the vendored OpenSSL; the exact file list.
```bash
#!/bin/bash
# Tests scripts/build-deb.sh without building: its syntax, and --control (DEBIAN/control for given values; runs on any
# OS). The real build and its gates run on Linux (CI's deb jobs, the VM).
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
SCRIPT="$PWD/scripts/build-deb.sh"

PASS=0; FAIL=0
ok()  { PASS=$((PASS+1)); echo "ok   - $1"; }
nok() { FAIL=$((FAIL+1)); echo "FAIL - $1"; }
check() { local desc="$1"; shift; if "$@"; then ok "$desc"; else nok "$desc"; fi; }
has() { grep -qF -- "$2" <<<"$1"; }
lacks() { ! grep -qF -- "$2" <<<"$1"; }

check "bash -n" bash -n "$SCRIPT"
if command -v shellcheck >/dev/null 2>&1; then check "shellcheck" shellcheck "$SCRIPT" "$0"; else echo "skip - shellcheck is not installed"; fi

C="$("$SCRIPT" --control 2.1.0 arm64 'libc6 (>= 2.39), libwebkit2gtk-4.1-0 (>= 2.44)')"
check "control: the package name exactly" has "$C" $'\nPackage: linvpn\n'
check "control: starts with Package" test "$(head -n 1 <<<"$C")" = "Package: linvpn"
check "control: version" has "$C" "Version: 2.1.0"
check "control: architecture" has "$C" "Architecture: arm64"
check "control: shlibs first" has "$C" "Depends: libc6 (>= 2.39), libwebkit2gtk-4.1-0 (>= 2.44), network-manager, curl, pkexec | policykit-1"
check "control: a StatusNotifierItem host" has "$C" "gnome-shell-extension-appindicator | plasma-workspace"
check "control: the NM plugins recommended" has "$C" "Recommends: network-manager-openvpn-gnome | network-manager-openvpn"
check "control: strongSwan and vpnc too" bash -c "grep -q 'network-manager-strongswan' <<<'$C' && grep -q 'network-manager-vpnc-gnome | network-manager-vpnc' <<<'$C'"
check "control: refuses a bad version" bash -c "! '$SCRIPT' --control 2.1 amd64 '' >/dev/null 2>&1"
check "control: refuses an unknown architecture" bash -c "! '$SCRIPT' --control 2.1.0 i386 '' >/dev/null 2>&1"
check "no maintainer scripts are ever staged" bash -c "! grep -nE 'DEBIAN/(pre|post)(inst|rm)' '$SCRIPT'"
check "the gate refuses maintainer scripts" grep -qF 'no maintainer scripts allowed' "$SCRIPT"

echo "$PASS passed, $FAIL failed"
[ "$FAIL" -eq 0 ]
```

```bash
#!/bin/bash
# Builds LinVPN's .deb on Linux, the counterpart of build-dmg.sh and build-msi.sh (LinVPN plan, Task 21). One
# architecture per run, this machine's (`dpkg --print-architecture`: amd64 or arm64); CI runs it on an amd64 and an
# arm64 runner (ubuntu-24.04, so the package runs on Ubuntu 24.04+ and Debian 13).
#   1. the three version numbers agree; no build-changing variable leaks in; THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.linux.txt current
#   2. the UI, the release app (`tauri build --no-bundle`: custom-protocol, tauri.linux.conf.json → target/release/
#      linvpn) and the root update step (the helper crate's linvpn-install-update)
#   3. release gates on both binaries: the ELF machine; no debug-only code (MACVPN_SANDBOX_HOME, MACVPN_SMOKE);
#      SQLCipher on the vendored OpenSSL 3, statically linked (no libssl, libcrypto or libsqlite3 among the NEEDED
#      libraries; OpenSSL 3's version string inside); the root step needs only libc, libm, libgcc_s and the loader
#   4. the staged tree, DEBIAN/control (Package linvpn exactly, Version, Architecture, Depends from dpkg-shlibdeps plus
#      the fixed list, Recommends: the NM plugins) and md5sums; no maintainer scripts; dpkg-deb --root-owner-group
#   5. the built .deb read back: its three fields, only control and md5sums as control files, every file root:root,
#      the file list (path and mode) equal to scripts/bundle-baseline/linvpn-deb-files.txt, the two binaries byte for
#      byte the gated ones; .sha256 beside it
#
#   scripts/build-deb.sh [--write-baseline]
#   scripts/build-deb.sh --control VERSION ARCH SHLIBS   only print DEBIAN/control for these values, then exit
#                                                        (scripts/test-build-deb.sh; runs on any OS)
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."

die() { echo "error: $*" >&2; exit 1; }

DEPENDS_FIXED="network-manager, curl, pkexec | policykit-1, gnome-shell-extension-appindicator | plasma-workspace"
RECOMMENDS="network-manager-openvpn-gnome | network-manager-openvpn, network-manager-l2tp-gnome | network-manager-l2tp, network-manager-strongswan, network-manager-sstp-gnome | network-manager-sstp, network-manager-vpnc-gnome | network-manager-vpnc"

control() { # VERSION ARCH SHLIBS
  [[ "$1" =~ ^[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$ ]] || die "bad version: $1"
  [[ "$2" =~ ^(amd64|arm64)$ ]] || die "bad architecture: $2"
  local depends="$DEPENDS_FIXED"
  [ -z "$3" ] || depends="$3, $depends"
  cat <<CONTROL
Package: linvpn
Version: $1
Architecture: $2
Maintainer: SepsiG <SepsiG@users.noreply.github.com>
Section: net
Priority: optional
Homepage: https://github.com/SepsiG/MacVPN
Depends: $depends
Recommends: $RECOMMENDS
Description: VPN connections in the top bar
 LinVPN keeps VPN connections (OpenVPN, WireGuard, L2TP/IPsec, IKEv2, Cisco IPsec
 and SSTP) in an encrypted vault and connects them through NetworkManager. The
 Linux sibling of MacVPN and WinVPN.
CONTROL
}

if [ "${1:-}" = --control ]; then
  [ $# -eq 4 ] || die "--control VERSION ARCH SHLIBS"
  control "$2" "$3" "$4"
  exit 0
fi

WRITE_BASELINE=0
case "${1:-}" in
  "") ;;
  --write-baseline) WRITE_BASELINE=1 ;;
  *) die "unknown option: $1" ;;
esac
[ "$(uname -s)" = Linux ] || die "build the .deb on Linux"
ARCH="$(dpkg --print-architecture)"
case "$ARCH" in
  amd64) ELF_MACHINE="X86-64" ;;
  arm64) ELF_MACHINE="AArch64" ;;
  *) die "unsupported architecture: $ARCH" ;;
esac
for tool in dpkg-deb dpkg-shlibdeps readelf md5sum sha256sum node npm cargo; do
  command -v "$tool" >/dev/null 2>&1 || die "$tool is missing (build-essential, dpkg-dev, binutils, nodejs)"
done

echo "==> 1. versions, environment, notices"
V_CARGO="$(sed -n 's/^version = "\(.*\)"$/\1/p' Cargo.toml | head -n 1)"
V_CONF="$(node -p "require('./app/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json').version")"
V_UI="$(node -p "require('./app/ui/package.json').version")"
[ "$V_CARGO" = "$V_CONF" ] && [ "$V_CONF" = "$V_UI" ] || die "versions differ: Cargo $V_CARGO, tauri.conf.json $V_CONF, package.json $V_UI"
VERSION="$V_CARGO"
for v in MACVPN_SANDBOX_HOME MACVPN_SMOKE_DIR RUSTFLAGS CARGO_ENCODED_RUSTFLAGS CARGO_BUILD_TARGET OPENSSL_DIR \
  OPENSSL_LIB_DIR OPENSSL_INCLUDE_DIR OPENSSL_STATIC OPENSSL_NO_VENDOR SQLCIPHER_LIB_DIR LIBSQLITE3_SYS_USE_PKG_CONFIG; do
  [ -z "${!v:-}" ] || die "$v must not be set for a release build"
done
node scripts/third-party-notices.mjs --os linux --check \
  || die "THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.linux.txt is not current (node scripts/third-party-notices.mjs --os linux)"

echo "==> 2. the UI, the app and the root update step"
(cd app/ui && npm ci --no-audit --no-fund && npm run build)
(cd app/ui && npm run tauri -- build --no-bundle)
cargo build --release --locked -p macvpn-helper --bin linvpn-install-update
APP=target/release/linvpn
STEP=target/release/linvpn-install-update
[ -x "$APP" ] || die "no $APP (tauri.linux.conf.json's mainBinaryName must be linvpn)"
[ -x "$STEP" ] || die "no $STEP"

echo "==> 3. release gates on the binaries"
needed() { readelf -d "$1" | sed -n 's/.*(NEEDED).*\[\(.*\)\]/\1/p' | sort; }
for bin in "$APP" "$STEP"; do
  readelf -h "$bin" | grep -q "Machine:.*$ELF_MACHINE" || die "$bin is not $ELF_MACHINE"
  for marker in MACVPN_SANDBOX_HOME MACVPN_SMOKE; do
    if grep -aq "$marker" "$bin"; then die "$bin contains the debug-only $marker"; fi
  done
done
if needed "$APP" | grep -Eq '^lib(ssl|crypto|sqlite3)\.so'; then die "the app links a system libssl, libcrypto or libsqlite3"; fi
grep -aq 'OpenSSL 3\.' "$APP" || die "no vendored OpenSSL 3 in the app (SQLCipher's crypto provider)"
for lib in $(needed "$STEP"); do
  case "$lib" in
    libc.so.6|libm.so.6|libgcc_s.so.1|ld-linux-*.so.*) ;;
    *) die "the root update step links $lib" ;;
  esac
done

echo "==> 4. the package tree"
STAGE="target/linvpn-deb/$ARCH"
ROOT="$STAGE/root"
rm -rf "$STAGE"
install -D -m 0755 "$APP" "$ROOT/usr/bin/linvpn"
install -D -m 0755 "$STEP" "$ROOT/usr/libexec/linvpn/linvpn-install-update"
install -D -m 0644 packaging/linux/linvpn.desktop "$ROOT/usr/share/applications/linvpn.desktop"
install -D -m 0644 packaging/linux/linvpn-mime.xml "$ROOT/usr/share/mime/packages/linvpn.xml"
install -D -m 0644 packaging/linux/local.linvpn.update.policy "$ROOT/usr/share/polkit-1/actions/local.linvpn.update.policy"
install -D -m 0644 app/src-tauri/icons/32x32.png "$ROOT/usr/share/icons/hicolor/32x32/apps/linvpn.png"
install -D -m 0644 app/src-tauri/icons/128x128.png "$ROOT/usr/share/icons/hicolor/128x128/apps/linvpn.png"
install -D -m 0644 "app/src-tauri/icons/128x128@2x.png" "$ROOT/usr/share/icons/hicolor/256x256/apps/linvpn.png"
install -D -m 0644 packaging/linux/copyright "$ROOT/usr/share/doc/linvpn/copyright"
install -D -m 0644 THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.linux.txt "$ROOT/usr/share/doc/linvpn/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt"
if command -v desktop-file-validate >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  desktop-file-validate "$ROOT/usr/share/applications/linvpn.desktop" || die "the desktop entry is invalid"
fi
mkdir -p "$STAGE/shlibs/debian"
printf 'Source: linvpn\n\nPackage: linvpn\nArchitecture: any\n' > "$STAGE/shlibs/debian/control"
ABS_ROOT="$(cd "$ROOT" && pwd)"
SHLIBS="$(cd "$STAGE/shlibs" && dpkg-shlibdeps -O "$ABS_ROOT/usr/bin/linvpn" "$ABS_ROOT/usr/libexec/linvpn/linvpn-install-update" 2>/dev/null \
  | sed -n 's/^shlibs:Depends=//p')"
[ -n "$SHLIBS" ] || die "dpkg-shlibdeps found no library dependencies"
mkdir -p "$ROOT/DEBIAN"
control "$VERSION" "$ARCH" "$SHLIBS" > "$ROOT/DEBIAN/control"
(cd "$ROOT" && find usr -type f -print0 | sort -z | xargs -0 md5sum) > "$ROOT/DEBIAN/md5sums"
chmod 0644 "$ROOT/DEBIAN/control" "$ROOT/DEBIAN/md5sums"
find "$ROOT" -type d -exec chmod 0755 {} +
OUT=dist/linux
mkdir -p "$OUT"
DEB="$OUT/linvpn_${VERSION}_${ARCH}.deb"
rm -f "$DEB" "$DEB.sha256"
dpkg-deb --root-owner-group -Zxz --build "$ROOT" "$DEB" >/dev/null

echo "==> 5. the built package"
[ "$(dpkg-deb --field "$DEB" Package)" = linvpn ] || die "Package is not linvpn"
[ "$(dpkg-deb --field "$DEB" Version)" = "$VERSION" ] || die "Version is not $VERSION"
[ "$(dpkg-deb --field "$DEB" Architecture)" = "$ARCH" ] || die "Architecture is not $ARCH"
CTRL="$(dpkg-deb --ctrl-tarfile "$DEB" | tar -t | sort | tr '\n' ' ')"
[ "$CTRL" = "./ ./control ./md5sums " ] || die "unexpected control files (no maintainer scripts allowed): $CTRL"
LIST="$(dpkg-deb --fsys-tarfile "$DEB" | tar -tv | awk '{print $1, $2, $6}' | sort -k3)"
if awk '$2 != "root/root"' <<<"$LIST" | grep -q .; then die "a file is not owned by root:root"; fi
FILES="$(awk '{print $1, $3}' <<<"$LIST")"
BASELINE=scripts/bundle-baseline/linvpn-deb-files.txt
if [ "$WRITE_BASELINE" = 1 ]; then
  printf '%s\n' "$FILES" > "$BASELINE"
  echo "wrote $BASELINE"
fi
diff -u "$BASELINE" <(printf '%s\n' "$FILES") || die "the package's files differ from $BASELINE (--write-baseline after a deliberate change)"
X="$STAGE/extract"
dpkg-deb -x "$DEB" "$X"
cmp -s "$X/usr/bin/linvpn" "$APP" || die "the packaged linvpn is not the gated one"
cmp -s "$X/usr/libexec/linvpn/linvpn-install-update" "$STEP" || die "the packaged root step is not the gated one"
(cd "$OUT" && sha256sum "$(basename "$DEB")" > "$(basename "$DEB").sha256")
echo "OK: $DEB"
cat "$DEB.sha256"
```

Run (Mac): `chmod +x scripts/build-deb.sh && scripts/test-build-deb.sh` → `… passed, 0 failed`; the `$VAR`-before-non-ASCII rule: `perl -ne 'print "$ARGV:$.: $_" if /\$[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*[\x80-\xff]/' scripts/build-deb.sh scripts/test-build-deb.sh` → no output.

```bash
LINVPN_REMOTE_DIR=linvpn-work-slot1 scripts/lin-remote.sh fetch scripts/bundle-baseline/linvpn-deb-files.txt scripts/bundle-baseline
LINVPN_REMOTE_DIR=linvpn-work-slot1 scripts/lin-remote.sh fetch dist/linux ./dist
```

Run again without `--write-baseline` (the gate itself):

```bash
git commit -m "build: LinVPN's .deb with its release gates" -m "dpkg-deb from the gated release binaries; Package linvpn, Depends from dpkg-shlibdeps plus NetworkManager, curl, pkexec and an SNI host, the NM plugins as Recommends; no maintainer scripts; the file list pinned." -m "Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>"
```

- [ ] **Step 8: Tell the maintainer (CP-deb install)**

> **CP-deb install.** The package is in `dist/linux/linvpn_2.0.5_<arch>.deb` on the VM's checkout
> (`~/linvpn-work-slot1/dist/linux/`). At the VM, as your admin user:
>
> ```bash
> sudo apt-get install /home/linvpn-dev/linvpn-work-slot1/dist/linux/linvpn_2.0.5_<arch>.deb
> dpkg-query --show linvpn
> ls -l /usr/bin/linvpn /usr/libexec/linvpn/linvpn-install-update /usr/share/polkit-1/actions/local.linvpn.update.policy
> ```
>
> (If apt cannot read the file from that home folder, copy it to `/tmp` first with `cp` and install from there.)
> Then log in at the desktop as `linvpn-dev`, start **LinVPN** from the app grid and tell the coordinator: the
> tray icon appears; the vault-create window opens; after creating the vault, quitting and starting LinVPN again,
> it opens without asking for the master password (the key is in the keyring); a `.ovpn` file double-clicked in
> Files opens in LinVPN.
